\ell_{2|1}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})
which approximates it uniformly well across its domain.
We control the change in the value of the min-max problem incurred by this replacement.
The objective
\ell_{2|1}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})
is introduced because it is easier to analyze.

2.

Ideally, we would evaluate the internal maximization exactly.
However, doing so explicitly leads to complicated expressions.
Instead, we construct a lower bound on the internal maximization.
In particular, for any function
\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v})
for which
\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v})\in\mathcal{S}_{u}
, the quantity
\ell_{2|1}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v}),\boldsymbol{v})
is a lower bound on the value of the maximization problem.
Our strategy is to pick a function
\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v})
so that the function
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v}):=\ell_{2|1}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v}),\boldsymbol{v})
is tractable to analyze and provides a good enough lower bound for our purposes.

3.

We establish several properties of the lower bound
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v})
.
In particular, we show
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})
is close to
\ell_{2|1}^{*}
,
the subdifferential

\partial\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})
contains a small element, and
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v})
is strongly convex for bounded
\boldsymbol{v}
.

4.

Using standard convex analysis techniques,
these properties imply a lower bound on the min-max problem over
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq C_{v}
and over
\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v|1}^{c}(\epsilon)\cap\{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq C_{v}\}
.

We now carry out these steps in detail.

Step 1: we replace
\ell_{2|1}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})
by a function
\ell_{2|1}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})
which approximates it uniformly well across its domain.

We may use a Gram-Schmidt construction to replace
\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}
,
\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}
, and
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}
by
\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}
,
\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}
, and
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}
so that

\boldsymbol{T}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Big)=\operatorname{diag}(\tau_{e_{2}}^{2},1,1),

(165)

and

\max\Big\{\frac{\|\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\frac{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}-\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Big\}<C^{\prime}\epsilon.

(166)

This follows by Eqs. (
162
) and (
321
) and Lemma
M.2
.
Define

\boldsymbol{Z}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\begin{pmatrix}|&|&|\\[4.0pt]
\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}/\tau_{e_{2}}&\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}&\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}\\[4.0pt]
|&|&|\end{pmatrix}.

(167)

By the Gram-Schmidt construction,
\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}=\mathbf{I}_{3}
.

We next apply a Gram-Schmidt step to
\boldsymbol{u}_{1}
.
In particular,
define

\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}=\boldsymbol{Z}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{n_{12}}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}\zeta_{1}\tau_{e_{1}}\rho_{e}\\[4.0pt]
\frac{n_{12}}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}\zeta_{1}\tau_{h_{1}}\\[4.0pt]
0\end{pmatrix}+\alpha_{\perp}\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}},

(168)

where we define

\alpha^{2}:=\frac{n_{12}}{n_{1}}\zeta_{1}^{2}(\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}+\tau_{h_{1}}^{2})=n_{12}\zeta_{1}^{2}\tau_{g_{1}}^{2},\qquad\alpha_{\|}^{2}:=\frac{n_{12}^{2}}{n_{1}n_{2}}\zeta_{1}^{2}(\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}\rho_{e}^{2}+\tau_{h_{1}}^{2}),\qquad\alpha_{\perp}^{2}:=\alpha^{2}-\alpha_{\|}^{2}.

(169)

If
\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}=0
, we may replace
\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}}
with any unit vector orthogonal to the column space of
\boldsymbol{Z}
, and all computations to come will remain valid.
Note that
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}=\alpha
,
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}=\alpha_{\|}
, and
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}=\alpha_{\perp}
.
Our goal is to show

\frac{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}<C^{\prime}\epsilon.

(170)

Because there is no
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}},\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{regr}}
, and regression method dependent constant
c>0
such that
\alpha>c
, we cannot apply Lemma
M.2
to bound this quantity. (Indeed, under assumption
A1
and
A2
we can even have
n_{1}2=0
, in which case
\alpha=0
).
Thus, to bound this difference, we require a more careful argument.

First observe
3
3

3

This part of the argument is essentially the same as in the proof of Lemma
M.2
.

\displaystyle\frac{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}-\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}=\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\zeta_{1}\tau_{e_{1}}\rho_{e}n_{12}}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}\\[4.0pt]
\frac{\zeta_{1}n_{12}}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}\\[4.0pt]
0\end{pmatrix}-\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}\right\|_{2}

(171)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\zeta_{1}\tau_{e_{1}}\rho_{e}n_{12}}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}\\[4.0pt]
\frac{\zeta_{1}n_{12}}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}\\[4.0pt]
0\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}},\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}\,\tau_{e_{2}}}\\[4.0pt]
\frac{\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}},\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}\\[4.0pt]
\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}},\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}},\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}\,\tau_{e_{2}}}\\[4.0pt]
\frac{\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}},\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}\\[4.0pt]
\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}},\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}},\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}\,\tau_{e_{2}}}\\[4.0pt]
\frac{\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}},\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}\\[4.0pt]
\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}},\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{2}<C^{\prime}\epsilon,

where we bound the first term on the right-hand side by Eqs. (
162
) and (
321
),
and we bound the second term by Eq. (
166
).

Thus, to show
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}<C^{\prime}\epsilon
, it now suffices to show
\big|\alpha_{\perp}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}\big|<C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
The previous display implies that
\big|\alpha_{\|}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}\big|<C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
Because, by Eq. (
162
), we also have

\left|\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{\|}\\[4.0pt]
\alpha_{\perp}\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{2}-\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}\\[4.0pt]
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{2}\right|=\left|\alpha-\frac{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}\right|\leq C^{\prime}\epsilon,

(172)

we conclude

\left|\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{\|}\\[4.0pt]
\alpha_{\perp}\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{2}-\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{\|}\\[4.0pt]
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{2}\right|<C^{\prime}\epsilon.

(173)

Thus,

\left|\sqrt{\alpha_{\|}^{2}+\Big(\alpha_{\perp}+\frac{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}-\alpha_{\perp}\Big)^{2}}-\sqrt{\alpha_{\|}^{2}+\alpha_{\perp}^{2}}\right|\leq C^{\prime}\epsilon.

(174)

It is straightforward to check that for
\alpha_{\perp}/\alpha_{\|}>c^{\prime}
,
the function
f(x)\mapsto\sqrt{\alpha_{\|}^{2}+(\alpha_{\perp}+x)^{2}}
satisfies
|f(x)-f(0)|>c^{\prime\prime}|x|
for
x\in[-\alpha_{\perp},\infty)
, where
c^{\prime\prime}>0
depends only on
c^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
C.1
,

\frac{\alpha_{\|}}{\alpha}\leq\sqrt{\frac{n_{12}}{n_{2}}}\sqrt{\frac{\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}\rho_{e}^{2}+\tau_{h_{1}}^{2}}{\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}+\tau_{h_{1}}^{2}}}<1-c\quad\text{whence}\quad\frac{\alpha_{\perp}}{\alpha_{\|}}\geq c^{\prime}.

(175)

Thus, we conclude that
c^{\prime\prime}\big|\alpha_{\perp}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}\big|<C^{\prime}\epsilon
, as desired.
Eq. (
170
) follows.

Finally, by Eq. (
162
),
we can replace
\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}^{c}}
by
\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}^{c}}^{\mathrm{gs}}
so that
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}^{c}}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}=\sqrt{n_{1}-n_{12}}\,\tau_{g_{1}}\zeta_{1}
and
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}^{c}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}^{c}}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}<\epsilon
.
We define
\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}
to be
\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}
on
\mathcal{I}_{2}
and
\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}^{c}}^{\mathrm{gs}}
on
\mathcal{I}_{2}^{c}
.
The above discussion implies that
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}<C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
Because
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}=\sqrt{n_{1}}\zeta_{1}\tau_{g_{1}}>c
,
we conclude that also
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}/\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}-\boldsymbol{u}_{1}/\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}\|_{2}<C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
Our Gram-Schmidt constructions are now complete.

Define

\displaystyle\ell_{2|1}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{n_{2}}\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{cg}}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u}),\boldsymbol{v}\rangle+\frac{1}{n_{2}}\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{\mathrm{cg},\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{v}),\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle+\frac{1}{n_{2}}\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}-\frac{1}{2{n_{2}}}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}^{2}+\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}),

(176)

where

\begin{gathered}\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{cg}}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u}):=\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}},\boldsymbol{u}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g},\qquad\boldsymbol{h}_{\mathrm{cg},\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{v}):=\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}.\end{gathered}

(177)

Observe

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{2}}(\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{cg}}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u})-\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{cg}}(\boldsymbol{u}))=\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Big\langle\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}}-\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}},\frac{\boldsymbol{u}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Big\rangle+\frac{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Big(\frac{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}-\frac{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Big)+\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\frac{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}.

(178)

By Eqs. (
162
) and (
321
),
we have
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}\leq C\sqrt{p/n_{2}}\leq C
,
\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}\|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{p/n_{2}}\leq C
, and
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}\|/\sqrt{n_{2}}\leq C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
By the construction of
\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}
and the discussion above,
we have

\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}-\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}}^{\perp}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{2}\big\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}/\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}-\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}/\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}\big\|_{2}\leq C\epsilon
,
so that for
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}\leq C_{u}
we have
\|\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{cg}}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u})-\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{cg}}(\boldsymbol{u})\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}<C^{\prime}\,\epsilon
.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}(\boldsymbol{h}_{\mathrm{cg}}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u})-\boldsymbol{h}_{\mathrm{cg}}(\boldsymbol{u}))=\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}}\cdot\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h}^{\mathrm{gs}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\cdot\frac{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}^{\mathrm{gs}}-\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}.

(179)

By Eqs. (
162
) and (
321
),
we have
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h}^{\mathrm{gs}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}\leq C
,
\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}^{\mathrm{gs}}-\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}\leq C
, and
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}\leq C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
Thus, for
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{p/n_{2}}
we have
\|\boldsymbol{h}_{\mathrm{cg}}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u})-\boldsymbol{h}_{\mathrm{cg}}(\boldsymbol{u})\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}<C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
Finally, by Eq. (
166
),

\big|\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\big|/n_{2}\leq C^{\prime}\epsilon
for
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}<C_{u}
.
Combining the preceding results,
we conclude

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathcal{S}_{u|1}\end{subarray}}\;\sup_{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq C_{v}}|\ell_{2|1}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})-\ell_{2|1}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})|<\epsilon.

(180)

Define

\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}):=\begin{pmatrix}\tau_{e_{2}}\\[3.0pt]
\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}\rangle/\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}\\[3.0pt]
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\end{pmatrix}.

(181)

We may rewrite

\ell_{2|1}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})-\frac{1}{n_{2}}\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{cg}}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u}),\boldsymbol{v}\rangle-\frac{1}{2{n_{2}}}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}+\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}).

(182)

Step 2: we construct a lower bound
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v})
on the internal maximization.

To do so, we define
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v})=\ell_{2|1}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v}),\boldsymbol{v})
for some
\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v})\in\mathcal{S}_{u}
.
To motivate our choice of
\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v})
,
recall that in the fixed-design model (see Eq. (
64
))

\begin{gathered}\frac{1}{n_{2}}\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]=n_{2}\zeta_{2}^{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2},\\
\frac{\mathbb{E}[\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f},\boldsymbol{u}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}\rangle]}{\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]^{1/2}\sqrt{n_{2}}}=\frac{n_{12}\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}\tau_{\hat{e}_{1}^{\mathrm{d}}}\tau_{\hat{e}_{2}^{\mathrm{d}}}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}}{\sqrt{n_{12}n_{2}}\zeta_{1}\tau_{\hat{e}_{1}^{\mathrm{d}}}}=\sqrt{\frac{n_{12}}{n_{2}}}\zeta_{2}\tau_{\hat{e}_{2}^{\mathrm{d}}}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}=\sqrt{n_{12}}\zeta_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}},\end{gathered}

(183)

where we use that
\tau_{\hat{e}_{2}^{\mathrm{d}}}=\sqrt{n_{2}}\tau_{g_{2}}
(see Eq. (
62
)).
We choose
\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v})
to be maximally aligned with
\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})
subject to the empirical counterpart of these equations holding with equality and
\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v})
having support restricted to
\mathcal{I}_{2}
.
Namely,
we maximize
\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}(\boldsymbol{v})^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})
subject to

\|\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v})\|_{2}^{2}/n_{2}=n_{2}\zeta_{2}^{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}
,

\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}/\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2},\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}(\boldsymbol{v})/\sqrt{n_{2}}\rangle=\sqrt{n_{12}}\,\zeta_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}
, and
\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}^{c}}(\boldsymbol{v})=0
.
This gives

\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}(\boldsymbol{v})}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}:=\sqrt{n_{12}}\,\zeta_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}}+\sqrt{n_{2}\zeta_{2}^{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}-n_{12}\zeta_{2}^{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}^{2}}\,\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})\|_{2}},

(184)

where we adopt the convention that
\boldsymbol{0}/\|\boldsymbol{0}\|_{2}=\boldsymbol{0}
.
The projection
\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}}^{\perp}
is a projection in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{2}}
not in
{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
.

We define
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v})=\ell_{2|1}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v}),\boldsymbol{v})
.
By design,
\|\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v})\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}=\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\zeta_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}<C
by Lemma
C.1
.
We immediately get that
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v})
is a lower-bound on the inner maximization:

\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v|1}^{c}(\Delta)\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq R\end{subarray}}\;\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathcal{S}_{u|1}}\ell_{2|1}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\geq\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{v}\in E(\Delta)\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq R\end{subarray}}\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v}).

(185)

We now write
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v})
explicitly.
One simplification arising from our choice of
\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v})
(and a primary reason for our choice) is that
\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{cg}}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v}))
and
\|\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v})\|_{2}^{2}/n_{2}
do not depend on
\boldsymbol{v}
, so that the term
\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{cg}}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v})),\boldsymbol{v}\rangle
has a straightforward linear dependence on
\boldsymbol{v}
.
In particular,
due the Gram-Schmidt construction, we have
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}/\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}=\sqrt{n_{12}/n_{1}}
, whence

\frac{1}{n_{2}}\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}},\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v})\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}},\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}(\boldsymbol{v})\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}}}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\sqrt{n_{12}}\zeta_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}\sqrt{\frac{n_{12}}{n_{1}}}=\zeta_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g},

(186)

where we have used that
\rho_{g}=(n_{12}/\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}})\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}
(see Eq. (
62
)).
Recalling the definition of the conditional fixed design model (
150
), we have

\begin{gathered}\frac{1}{n_{2}^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v})\|_{2}^{2}=\zeta_{2}^{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2},\quad\frac{1}{n_{2}}\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v})\|_{2}=\sqrt{\zeta_{2}^{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}}\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v})\|_{2}^{2}/n_{2}^{2}}=\sqrt{\zeta_{2}^{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}-\zeta_{2}^{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\rho_{g}^{2}}=\zeta_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}^{\perp},\\
\frac{1}{n_{2}}\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{cg}}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u}(\boldsymbol{v}))=\tau_{g_{2}}\zeta_{2}(\rho_{g}\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}+\rho_{g}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g})=\zeta_{2}\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{cf}.\end{gathered}

(187)

Thus,

\displaystyle\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v})=\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{12}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}},\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}}+\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}-n_{12}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\Big(\|\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}},\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})\rangle^{2}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}^{2}}\Big)^{1/2}

(188)

\displaystyle-\zeta_{2}\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{cf\top}\boldsymbol{v}-\frac{n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\zeta_{2}^{2}}{2}+\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}).

By the Gram-Schmidt construction (see Eq. (
168
) and recall
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}=\sqrt{n_{12}}\,\zeta_{1}\tau_{g_{1}}
),
we have

\boldsymbol{a}:=\frac{\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{\frac{n_{12}}{n_{2}}}\,\frac{\tau_{e_{1}}\rho_{e}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}\,\tau_{g_{1}}}\\[4.0pt]
\sqrt{\frac{n_{12}}{n_{2}}}\frac{\tau_{h_{1}}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}\,\tau_{g_{1}}}\\[4.0pt]
0\end{pmatrix}.

(189)

Plugging this into the previous display and using that
\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}=\mathbf{I}_{3}
,
we conclude that

\displaystyle\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v})

\displaystyle=\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{12}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})+\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}-n_{12}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}^{2}}\,\|\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})\|_{\mathbf{I}-\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}}-\zeta_{2}\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{cf\top}\boldsymbol{v}-\frac{n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\zeta_{2}^{2}}{2}+\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}).

(190)

Note that by Eq. (
62
),
\|\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}^{2}=(n_{12}/(n_{1}n_{2}))(\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}\rho_{e}^{2}+\tau_{h_{1}}^{2})/\tau_{g_{1}}^{2}\leq(1/n_{1}))(\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}+\tau_{h_{1}}^{2})/\tau_{g_{1}}^{2}=1
, so that
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbf{I}-\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}}
is indeed a norm.

Step 3: we establish several properties of the lower bound
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v})
.

First, we show
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})
is close to
\ell_{2|1}^{*}
.
To do so, we will replace several quantities with those on which they concentrate.
In particular, we make replacements

\begin{gathered}\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{cf\top}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\longrightarrow\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\mathsf{df}_{2},\qquad\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\longrightarrow\omega_{2}.\end{gathered}

(191)

By Eqs. (
162
) and (
321
),
the term

\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{cf\top}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}
differs from its replacement by at most
C^{\prime}(p/n_{2})\epsilon<C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
The term
\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})
differs from its replacement by at most
C^{\prime}(p/n_{2})\epsilon<C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
The coefficient of
\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{cf\top}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}
is
\zeta_{2}\leq 1
.
The coefficient of
\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})
is 1.
Thus, the errors incurred by making these replacements in the expression for
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})
is at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
.

The quantity
\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})
involves the quantities
\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\rangle/\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}
and
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}
.
We replace these by

\begin{gathered}\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}}\longrightarrow\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h},\qquad\big\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\big\|_{2}\longrightarrow\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}^{\perp}.\end{gathered}

(192)

Recalling the definitions of
\boldsymbol{a}
and
\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})
,
these replacements induce the replacements

\begin{gathered}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\longrightarrow\sqrt{\frac{n_{12}}{n_{2}}}\frac{\tau_{e_{1}}\tau_{e_{2}}\rho_{e}+\tau_{h_{1}}\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}\tau_{g_{1}}}=\frac{\sqrt{n_{12}}\,(\tau_{\hat{e}_{1}^{\mathrm{d}}}/\sqrt{n_{1}})(\tau_{\hat{e}_{2}^{\mathrm{d}}}/\sqrt{n_{2}})\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}}{\tau_{g_{1}}}=\sqrt{n_{12}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}},\\
\|\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\|_{2}^{2}\longrightarrow\tau_{e_{2}}^{2}+\tau_{h_{2}}^{2}=n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2},\end{gathered}

(193)

where in the first line we have applied Eqs. (
62
) and (
63
), and in the second line we have applied Eq. (
61
).
By Eqs. (
162
) and Eq. (
321
),
the term
\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\rangle/\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}=\sqrt{p/n_{2}}\,\langle\sqrt{n_{1}/p}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\sqrt{n_{2}/p}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\rangle/\|\sqrt{n_{1}/p}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}
differs from its replacement by at most
C^{\prime}\sqrt{p/n_{2}}\,\epsilon<C^{\prime}\epsilon
,
where we use that the denominator
\|\sqrt{n_{1}/p}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}
concentrates on
\sqrt{n_{1}/p}\,\tau_{h_{1}}>c>0
.
The term
\big\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\big\|_{2}=(\|\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}^{2}-\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\rangle^{2}/\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}^{2})^{1/2}
differs from it replacement by at most
C^{\prime}\sqrt{p/n_{k}}\,\epsilon<C^{\prime}\epsilon
, where we use that
\rho_{h}^{\perp}>c
to bound the derivative of the square-root (See Lemma
C.1
).
Using Lemma
C.1
, we can check that the etnries of
\boldsymbol{a}
are bounded above by
C
,
whence
\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})
differs from its replacement by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
Using Lemma
C.1
again, we have
c<n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}<C
, whence
\|\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\|_{2}^{2}
differs from its replacement by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
These replacements induce the replacements

\begin{gathered}\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{12}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\longrightarrow\zeta_{2}n_{12}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}^{2},\\
\|\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\|_{\mathbf{I}-\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}}=\sqrt{\|\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\|_{2}^{2}-(\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}))^{2}}\longrightarrow\sqrt{n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}-n_{12}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}^{2}}.\end{gathered}

(194)

By Lemma
C.1
,
\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{12}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}\leq C
, whence the first of these replacements incurs an error of at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
By Lemma
C.1
,

n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}-n_{12}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}^{2}\geq cn_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\geq c
,
whence the second of these repalcements incurs an error of at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
.

Applying the definition of
\ell_{2|1}^{*}
(Eq. (
153
)) and
\zeta_{2}
(Eq. (
58
)) and performing some algebra,
we see that the replacements we have made for
\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{cf\top}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}
,
\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})
,
\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{12}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})
, and
\|\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\|_{\mathbf{I}-\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}}
induce the replacement of
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})
by the quantity
\ell_{2|1}^{*}
.
By the above discussion,

\big|\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})-\ell_{2|1}^{*}\big|<C^{\prime}\,\epsilon.

(195)

Second, we show
\partial\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})
contains a small element.
We compute

\displaystyle\partial\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})^{\top}\Big(\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{12}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}\boldsymbol{a}+\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}-n_{12}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}^{2}}\frac{(\mathbf{I}-\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top})\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})}{\|\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\|_{\mathbf{I}-\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}}}\Big)-\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{cf}+\partial\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})

(196)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}\Big(\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{12}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}-\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}-n_{12}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}^{2}}\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\|_{\mathbf{I}-\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}}}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}-n_{12}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}^{2}}}{\|\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\|_{\mathbf{I}-\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}}}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}-\zeta_{2}\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{cf}+\partial\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}),

where
\mathsf{D}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})
is the Jacobian of
\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})
,
given by

\mathsf{D}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})^{\top}=\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{0}&\frac{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}}&\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}}\end{pmatrix},

(197)

and in the second equality we have used
\mathsf{D}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})^{\top}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})=\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}
.
We make replacements

\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\rangle\longrightarrow\sqrt{n_{12}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}},\qquad\|\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\|_{\mathbf{I}-\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}}\longrightarrow\sqrt{n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}-n_{12}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}^{2}},

(198)

after which the first term becomes 0 and the subdifferential expression becomes

\partial\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\longrightarrow\zeta_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}-\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{cf})+\partial\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}).

(199)

The previous display is the subdifferential of the objective in Eq. (
151
) evaluated at its minimizer
\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}
. Thus, it contains
\boldsymbol{0}
.
As we have already shown,

\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\rangle
and
\|\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\|_{\mathbf{I}-\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}}
differ from their replacements by at most
C^{\prime}\,\epsilon
.
Our goal is to show that the subdifferential is also perturbed by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
Indeed,

\mathsf{D}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}\leq\|\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}=(n_{12}/n_{2})(\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}\rho_{e}+\tau_{h_{1}}^{2})/(n_{1}\tau_{g_{1}}^{2})\leq C
by Lemma
C.1
.
Also by Lemma
C.1
,

\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{12}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}\leq\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}}\tau_{g_{2}}\leq C
and
c<(n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}-n_{12}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}^{2})^{1/2}<C
.
By Eqs. (
162
) and (
321
) and Lemma
C.1
,

\|\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{p/n_{2}}\leq C
.
Thus, because
\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\rangle
concentrates on something bounded above by
C
,
\|\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\|_{\mathbf{I}-\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}}
concentrates on something bound below by
c
, and all relevant coefficients are bounded above by
C
,
the sub-differential set is perturbed in
\ell_{2}
by the replacements above by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
We conclude

\inf\big\{\|\boldsymbol{\delta}\|_{2}:\boldsymbol{\delta}\in\partial\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\big\}<C^{\prime}\,\epsilon.

(200)

Third, we show that
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v})
is
c
-strongly convex in a neighborhood of
\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}
.
Because
\bar{\Omega}_{2}
is convex,
\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})
is linear (because
a_{3}=0
), and
\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{cf\top}\boldsymbol{v}
is linear,
it suffices to show
\zeta_{2}(n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}-n_{12}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}^{2})^{1/2}\|\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})\|_{\mathbf{I}-\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}}
is strongly convex for
\boldsymbol{v}
bounded.
Because, as we have already justified,
\zeta_{2}(n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}-n_{12}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}^{2})^{1/2}>c
, it suffices to show that
\big\|\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})\big\|_{\mathbf{I}-\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}}
is
c
-strongly convex in
\boldsymbol{v}
.
For this
it is convenient to consider an alternative representation of the function
\|\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})\|_{\mathbf{I}-\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}}
.
Let
\boldsymbol{U}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times p}
be an orthonormal matrix whose first column is
\boldsymbol{v}_{1}/\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}
,
and let
\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{v}
.
Thus,
\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}
can be interpreted as
\boldsymbol{v}
after an orthogonal change of basis,
and
\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})=(\tau_{e_{2}},b^{\prime}_{1},\|\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}_{-1}\|_{2})^{\top}
.
Define
\breve{\boldsymbol{a}}=(\boldsymbol{a}^{\top},\boldsymbol{0}_{p-2}^{\top})^{\top}
,
\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}=(\tau_{e_{2}},\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime\top})^{\top}
, and
\boldsymbol{K}=\mathbf{I}_{p+1}-\breve{\boldsymbol{a}}\breve{\boldsymbol{a}}^{\top}
.
It is straightforward to show that
\|\boldsymbol{b}(\boldsymbol{v})\|_{\mathbf{I}-\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}}=\big\|\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}\big\|_{\boldsymbol{K}}
.

The Hessian of
\big\|\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}\big\|_{\boldsymbol{K}}
is

\boldsymbol{H}:=\frac{1}{\|\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}\|_{\boldsymbol{K}}}\boldsymbol{K}^{1/2}\Big(\mathbf{I}_{p+1}-\frac{\boldsymbol{K}^{1/2}\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}^{1/2}}{\|\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}\|_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{2}}\Big)\boldsymbol{K}^{1/2}.

(201)

We want to lower bound
\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime\top}\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime}
for all
\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime}=(0,\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top})^{\top}
with
\boldsymbol{\delta}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
,
\|\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime}\|_{2}=1
.
Because
n_{12}/n_{2}\leq 1
and

n_{1}\tau_{g_{1}}^{2}=\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}+\tau_{h_{k}}^{2}
(see Eq. (
62
)),

\|\breve{\boldsymbol{a}}\|_{2}^{2}=\|\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{n_{12}}{n_{1}n_{2}\tau_{g_{1}}^{2}}(\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}\rho_{e}^{2}+\tau_{h_{1}}^{2})\leq\frac{\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}\rho_{e}^{2}+\tau_{h_{1}}^{2}}{\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}+\tau_{h_{1}}^{2}}=1-\frac{\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}(1-\rho_{e}^{2})}{\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}+\tau_{h_{1}}^{2}},

(202)

whence

\boldsymbol{K}\succeq\frac{\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}(1-\rho_{e}^{2})}{\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}+\tau_{h_{1}}^{2}}\mathbf{I}_{p+1}\succeq c^{\prime}\mathbf{I}_{p+1},

(203)

where in the last step we use
c<\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}<C
,
\tau_{h_{1}}^{2}<C
, and
\rho_{e}^{2}<1-c
by Lemma
C.1
.
Next define
\cos_{\boldsymbol{K}}(\breve{\boldsymbol{v}},\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime})=\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime}/(\|\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}\|_{\boldsymbol{K}}\|\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime}\|_{\boldsymbol{K}})
.
Straightforward algebra gives for
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq C_{v}
that

\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime\top}\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime}=\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime}\|_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{2}}{\|\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}\|_{\boldsymbol{K}}}\Big(1-\cos_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{2}(\breve{\boldsymbol{v}},\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime})\Big)\geq\frac{4c^{\prime}\|\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime}\|_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{2}}{\|\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}\|_{\boldsymbol{K}}}\Big(1-\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime},\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}\rangle^{2}}{\|\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\|\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}\|_{2}^{2}}\Big)\geq c\Big(1-\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime},\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}\rangle^{2}}{\|\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\|\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}\|_{2}^{2}}\Big),

(204)

where the first inequality uses Lemma
M.3
and that
the condition number of
\boldsymbol{K}
is bounded by
1/c^{\prime}
,
and the second inequality uses that
\|\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime}\|_{2}=1
,
\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{K})\geq c
,
\sigma_{\max}(\boldsymbol{K})\leq 1
, and
\|\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}\|_{2}=\tau_{e_{2}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}=\tau_{e_{2}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C
.
Further, recalling the definition of
\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime},\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}
,

\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime},\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}\rangle^{2}}{\|\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\|\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}\|_{2}^{2}}=\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\delta},\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\rangle^{2}}{\tau_{e_{2}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}}\leq\frac{\|\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}}{\tau_{e_{2}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}}=\frac{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{\tau_{e_{2}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}}.

(205)

Using that
\tau_{e_{2}}^{2}>c
,
we have for
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq C_{v}
that
\langle\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime},\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}\rangle^{2}/(\|\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\|\breve{\boldsymbol{v}}\|_{2}^{2})\geq 1-c
.
Combining with the above bounds,
we conclude that

\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime\top}\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\prime}\geq c.

(206)

Thus, we conclude that
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v})
is
c
-strongly convex for
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq C_{v}
.

Step 4: we use these properties to establish lower bounds on the min-max problem.

Because
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v})
is
c
-strongly convex on
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq C_{v}
,
we have for any
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq C_{v}
and
\boldsymbol{\delta}\in\partial\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})
that

\displaystyle\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v})

\displaystyle\geq\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})+\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{v}-\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})+\frac{c}{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}-\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}^{2}

(207)

\displaystyle\geq\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})-\frac{16}{c}\|\boldsymbol{\delta}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{c}{4}\|\boldsymbol{v}-\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}^{2}.

Combining the previous display with Eqs. (
180
), (
185
), (
195
), and (
200
),
we conclude that

\begin{gathered}\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v|1}^{c}(\Delta)\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq C_{v}\end{subarray}}\;\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathcal{S}_{u|1}}\ell_{2|1}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\geq\ell_{2|1}^{*}-C^{\prime}\epsilon+\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v|1}^{c}(\Delta)\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq C_{v}\end{subarray}}\;\frac{c}{4}\|\boldsymbol{v}-\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}^{2},\\
\min_{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq C_{v}}\;\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathcal{S}_{u|1}}\ell_{2|1}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\geq\ell_{2|1}^{*}-C^{\prime}\epsilon.\end{gathered}

(208)

Further, by Eq. (
162
),

\big|\phi_{v}\big(\boldsymbol{v}\big)-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{v}\big(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\big)]\big|\leq\frac{\Delta}{2}.

(209)

By the definition of
E_{v|1}^{c}(\Delta)
and because
\phi_{v}
is
1
-Lipschitz,

\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v|1}^{c}(\Delta)\\
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq C_{v}\end{subarray}}\;\frac{c}{4}\|\boldsymbol{v}-\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}^{2}\geq c\frac{\Delta^{2}}{16}.

(210)

Combining Eq. (
208
) with the previous display and taking
\epsilon=c\Delta^{2}/(32C^{\prime})
(with the same values
c,C^{\prime}
appearing in Eq. (
208
)),
we conclude the lower bound inside the first probability in Eq. (
155
) holds.
The lower bound inside the second probability in Eq. (
155
) is given in Eq. (
208
).

In summary, we have shown that the lower bounds inside the probabilities in Eq. (
208
) hold on
\mathcal{G}_{2|1}(\epsilon,\sqrt{32C^{\prime}\epsilon/c})
for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
F.7
, we conclude that the probability bounds in Eq. (
208
) hold as well.

F.3.3
Upper bounds on the auxilliary max-min problem

In this section, we prove Eq. (
156
).
As in the previous section, we will show that our upper bound holds on the event
\mathcal{G}_{2|1}(\epsilon,\Delta)
for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
,
\Delta=\Delta(\epsilon)<c^{\prime}
, and
c^{\prime}
taken sufficiently small and depending only on
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}},\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{regr}}
, and the regression method.
We will henceforce assume we are on this event for
\epsilon,\Delta
for
c^{\prime}
sufficiently small, without repeatedly reminding the reader of this fact,
and all statements will be deterministic.

The major steps in the analysis are as in the previous section, except that the first and second steps occur in the opposite order.
We now carry out these steps in detail.

Step 1: we construct an upper bound on the internal minimization.

In particular, we evaluate
\ell_{2|1}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})
at
\boldsymbol{v}=\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}
.
Define

\ell_{2|1}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u}):=-\frac{1}{n_{2}}\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{cg}}(\boldsymbol{u}),\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\rangle+\frac{1}{n_{2}}\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{\mathrm{cg},\mathcal{I}_{2}}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}),\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle+\frac{1}{n_{2}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{2}-\frac{1}{2{n_{2}}}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}^{2}+\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}).

(211)

Indeed, by Eqs. (
162
) and (
321
) and Lemma
C.1
,
we have
\|\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{p/n_{k}}\leq C
, whence, for
C_{v}
chosen sufficiently large,

\min_{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq C_{v}}\ell_{2|1}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\leq\ell_{2|1}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u}).

(212)

Step 2: we replace
\ell_{2|1}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u})
by a function
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{u})
which approximates it uniformly well across its domain.

First, we remove the projections of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}
and
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}
.
That is, we replace the terms
\frac{1}{n_{2}}\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\langle\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\rangle
and
\frac{1}{n_{2}}\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\langle(\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h})_{\mathcal{I}_{2}},\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle
in the objective
\ell_{2|1}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u})
by
\frac{1}{n_{2}}\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\rangle
and
\frac{1}{n_{2}}\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}},\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle
.
These replacements induce the replacements

\begin{gathered}\frac{1}{n_{2}}\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{cg}}(\boldsymbol{u}),\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\rangle\longrightarrow\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{u}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}}}\frac{\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\rangle}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}+\frac{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\rangle}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\\
\frac{1}{n_{2}}\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{\mathrm{cg},\mathcal{I}_{2}}(\boldsymbol{u}),\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle\longrightarrow\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}}\frac{\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}},\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle}{n_{2}}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}},\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle}{n_{2}}.\end{gathered}

(213)

By Eqs. (
162
) and (
321
) and Lemma
C.1
,
we have
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}\leq C^{\prime}\epsilon
,
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}\leq C^{\prime}\epsilon
, and
\|\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}\leq C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
Thus, the value of the objective is perturbed by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
uniformly over

\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}\leq C_{u}
and
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq C_{v}
by this replacement.

Next, we make replacements

\begin{gathered}\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}^{\top}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\longrightarrow\frac{\tau_{g_{2}}\mathsf{df}_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\rho_{g},\qquad\frac{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}^{\top}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\longrightarrow\frac{\tau_{g_{2}}\mathsf{df}_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\rho_{g}^{\perp},\qquad\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}}\longrightarrow\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h},\qquad\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}\longrightarrow\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}^{\perp},\end{gathered}

(214)

which induce the replacements (recall the definition of
\boldsymbol{h}_{2}^{cf}
in Eq. (
150
))

\begin{gathered}\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{u}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}}}\frac{\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\rangle}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}+\frac{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\rangle}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\longrightarrow\frac{\tau_{g_{2}}\mathsf{df}_{2}}{n_{2}}\Bigg(\rho_{g}\,\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{u}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}}+\rho_{g}^{\perp}\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\Bigg)\\
\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}}\frac{\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}},\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle}{n_{2}}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}},\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle}{n_{2}}\longrightarrow\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf},\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle}{n_{2}}.\end{gathered}

(215)

By Eqs. (
162
) and (
321
),

\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}^{\top}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
and
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}^{\top}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
differ from their replacements by at most
C^{\prime}(p/n_{2})\epsilon\leq C^{\prime}\epsilon
;
and the term
\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\rangle/\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}=\sqrt{p/n_{2}}\langle\sqrt{n_{1}/p}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\sqrt{n_{2}/p}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\rangle/\|\sqrt{n_{1}/p}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}
differs from its replacement by at most
C^{\prime}\sqrt{p/n_{2}}\,\epsilon\leq C^{\prime}\epsilon
,
where we use that the denominator
\|\sqrt{n_{1}/p}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}
concentrates on
\sqrt{n_{1}/p}\,\tau_{h_{1}}>c
(see Lemma
C.1
).
Then
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}=\sqrt{p/n_{2}}((n_{2}/p)\|\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}^{2}-(n_{2}/p)\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\rangle^{2}/\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}^{2})^{1/2}
differs from its replacement by at most
C^{\prime}\sqrt{p/n_{2}}\,\epsilon
,
where we use that the quantity inside the square-root concentrates on
(n_{2}/p)\tau_{h_{2}}^{2}\rho_{h}^{\perp 2}\geq c
(see Lemma
C.1
).
Further, the coefficients of these terms are bounded by
C
.
Indeed,
the coefficients of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}^{\top}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
and
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}^{\top}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
are
\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{u}\rangle/(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}})
and
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
, which are bounded by
C_{u}
when
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}\leq C_{u}
.
The coefficients of
\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\rangle/\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}
and
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}
are
\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}/n_{2}
and
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}/n_{2}
,
which are bounded by
C
when
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}\leq C_{u}
because
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}\leq 2
and
\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}\leq 2
by Eqs. (
162
) and (
321
).
Thus, the value of the objective is perturbed by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
uniformly over

\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}\leq C_{u}
and
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq C_{v}
by these replacements.

Finally, we make the replacement
\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\longrightarrow\omega_{2}
.
This combined with all previous replacements gives the objective

\displaystyle\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{u})

\displaystyle:=-\frac{\tau_{g_{2}}\mathsf{df}_{2}}{n_{2}}\Bigg(\rho_{g}\,\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{u}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}}+\rho_{g}^{\perp}\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\Bigg)+\frac{1}{n_{2}}\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\top}\big(\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\boldsymbol{h}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}\big)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n_{2}}+\omega_{2}.

(216)

By Eq. (
162
),
\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})
differs from its replacement by at most
\epsilon
.
Together with the above discussion, we conclude

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\in E_{u|1}^{c}(\Delta)\\
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathcal{S}_{u|1}\end{subarray}}|\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{u})-\ell_{2|1}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{u})|<C^{\prime}\epsilon.

(217)

Step 3: we establish several properties of the upper bound
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{u})
.

First, we show
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf})
is close to
\ell_{2|1}^{*}
.
Recalling that
\boldsymbol{u}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}=\zeta_{2}(\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\boldsymbol{h}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf})
,
we compute

\displaystyle\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf})=-\frac{\tau_{g_{2}}\mathsf{df}_{2}}{n_{2}}\Bigg(\rho_{g}\,\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}}+\rho_{g}^{\perp}\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}\Bigg)+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{n_{2}}\big\|\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\boldsymbol{h}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}\big\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{\zeta_{2}^{2}}{2n_{2}}\big\|\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\boldsymbol{h}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}\big\|_{2}^{2}+\omega_{2}.

(218)

We make replacements

\frac{\big\|\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\boldsymbol{h}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{n_{2}}\longrightarrow n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2},\qquad\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}}}\longrightarrow\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g},\qquad\frac{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\longrightarrow\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}^{\perp}.

(219)

If we make these replacements, after some algebra the objective becomes
-\zeta_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\mathsf{df}_{2}+n_{2}\zeta_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}-\zeta_{2}^{2}n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}/2+\omega_{2}=n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\zeta_{2}^{2}+\omega_{2}=\ell_{2|1}^{*}
, where we have used that
n_{2}-\mathsf{df}_{2}=n_{2}\zeta_{2}
by the fixed point equation (
57
).

We bound the errors incurred by these replacements.
By Eq. (
150
), we may write the term
\|\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\boldsymbol{h}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}\|_{2}^{2}/n_{2}
as
\|\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}\|_{2}^{2}/n_{2}
.
Expanding the square and using that
\tau_{h_{2}}\leq C\sqrt{p/n_{2}}\leq C
by Lemma
C.1
,
we have by Eqs. (
162
) and (
321
) that
\|\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}\|_{2}^{2}/n_{2}
differs from
\tau_{e_{2}}^{2}+\tau_{h_{2}}^{2}
by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
Recall that by Eq. (
62
),
\tau_{e_{2}}^{2}+\tau_{h_{2}}^{2}=n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}
, whence
\|\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\boldsymbol{h}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}\|_{2}^{2}/n_{2}
differs from its replacement by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
Because the coefficients of this term are
\zeta_{2}
and
\zeta_{2}^{2}/2
, both of which are bound by
C
,
the objective is perturbed by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
by this replacement.

We now turn to the replacements of
\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\rangle/(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}})
and
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
.
Using the representation
\boldsymbol{u}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}=\zeta_{2}(\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}})
,
we write
\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\rangle/\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}=\zeta_{2}\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle/\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}+\zeta_{2}\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}},\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle/\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}+\zeta_{2}\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}^{\perp}\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\rangle/\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}
.
By Eqs. (
162
) and (
321
) and using that
\tau_{h_{2}}\leq C\sqrt{p/n_{2}}
(see Lemma
C.1
),
we conclude that
\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\rangle/\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}
differs from
\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}(n_{12}/\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}})(\tau_{e_{1}}\tau_{e_{2}}\rho_{e}+\tau_{h_{1}}\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h})
by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
Also by Eq. (
162
),
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}
differs from
\zeta_{1}\sqrt{n_{1}}\,\tau_{g_{1}}
by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
By Lemma
C.1
,
\zeta_{1}\sqrt{n_{1}}\,\tau_{g_{1}}>c
and
\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}(n_{12}/\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}})(\tau_{e_{1}}\tau_{e_{2}}\rho_{e}+\tau_{h_{1}}\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h})\leq C
,
so that we may combine the previous bounds to conclude that
\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\rangle/(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}})
differs
\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}(n_{12}/\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}})(\tau_{e_{1}}\tau_{e_{2}}\rho_{e}+\tau_{h_{1}}\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h})/(\zeta_{1}\sqrt{n_{1}}\,\tau_{g_{1}})
by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
Using Eq. (
62
),
we see that
\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}(n_{12}/\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}})(\tau_{e_{1}}\tau_{e_{2}}\rho_{e}+\tau_{h_{1}}\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h})/(\zeta_{1}\sqrt{n_{1}}\,\tau_{g_{1}})=\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}
,
whence
\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\rangle/(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}})
differs from its replacement by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
Its coefficient is
\tau_{g_{2}}\mathsf{df}_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}=\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}(1-\zeta_{2})\leq C
, where the equality uses the fixed point equation (
57
) and the inequality uses Lemma
C.1
.
Thus, the objective is perturbed by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
by this replacement.

We write

\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}=(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}^{2}/n_{2}-\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\rangle^{2}/(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}n_{2}))^{1/2}
.
Because
\boldsymbol{u}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}=\zeta_{2}(\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\boldsymbol{h}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf})
and
\boldsymbol{u}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}^{c}}=0
,
from the above discussion we have that
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}^{2}/n_{2}
differs from
\zeta_{2}^{2}n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}
by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
From the above bounds, we conclude that
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
differs from its replacement by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
,
where we use that the term inside the square-root is
\zeta_{2}^{2}n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}\rho_{g}^{\perp 2}>c
by Lemma
C.1
.

Combining all replacements, we conclude that

\Big|\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf})-\ell_{2|1}^{*}\Big|<C^{\prime}\epsilon.

(220)

Second, we show the gradient of
\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{u})
at
\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}
is small.
Because the coordinates
\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}^{c}}
are constrained to be 0, we only compute the gradient for coordinates in
\mathcal{I}_{2}
.
The gradient is

\displaystyle\nabla

\displaystyle\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf})_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}=-\frac{\tau_{g_{2}}\mathsf{df}_{2}}{n_{2}}\Bigg(\rho_{g}\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}}+\rho_{g}^{\perp}\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}}\Bigg)_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\frac{1}{n_{2}}\big(\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\boldsymbol{h}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}\big)-\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}}{n_{2}}

(221)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{-\frac{\tau_{g_{2}}\mathsf{df}_{2}}{n_{2}}\Big(\rho_{g}-\rho_{g}^{\perp}\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\rangle/\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}}\Big)\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}}}_{=:\boldsymbol{A}_{1}}+\underbrace{\Big(-\frac{\tau_{g_{2}}\mathsf{df}_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\rho_{g}^{\perp}\frac{1}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}}+\frac{1}{\zeta_{2}}-1\Big)\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}}{n_{2}}}_{=:\boldsymbol{A}_{2}},

where we use that
\boldsymbol{u}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}=\zeta_{2}(\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\boldsymbol{h}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf})
.
If
\mathcal{I}_{1}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}=\emptyset
,
then
\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}=0
and 0 appears in the denominator in the previous display.
In this case we interpret
\boldsymbol{u}_{1}/\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}=0
,
\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}^{\perp}=\mathbf{I}_{n_{2}}
.
Under this interpretation, all calculations to come will be correct.
Note that because we have already argued that
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}

differs from
\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}^{\perp}>c
by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
,
we have that
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}\neq 0
for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
, so that no other terms in the previous display will cause an issue.

We bound

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{A}_{1}\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\frac{\mathsf{df}_{2}}{n_{2}}\Bigg|\rho_{g}-\rho_{g}^{\perp}\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\rangle/\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}}\Bigg|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Bigg|\rho_{g}-\rho_{g}^{\perp}\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\rangle/\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}}\Bigg|,

(222)

where the inequality uses Lemma
C.1
and that
\mathsf{df}_{2}/n_{2}=1-\zeta_{2}
from the fixed point equation (
57
).
We make replacements
\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\rangle/(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}})\rightarrow\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}}\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}

and
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}\rightarrow\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}}\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}^{\perp}
,
after which the right-hand side becomes 0.
As we have already argued,
these terms differ by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
from their replacements.
Moreover,
\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}}\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}<C
and
\zeta_{2}\sqrt{n_{2}}\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}^{\perp}>c
by Lemma
C.1
.
We conclude

\big\|\boldsymbol{A}_{1}\big\|_{2}<\frac{C^{\prime}\epsilon}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}.

(223)

If we replace
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}

with
\sqrt{n_{2}}\tau_{g_{2}}\zeta_{2}\rho_{g}^{\perp}
in the expression for
\boldsymbol{A}_{2}
,
we get the quantity
\big(-\mathsf{df}_{2}/(\zeta_{2}n_{2})+1/\zeta_{2}-1\big)\boldsymbol{u}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}/\sqrt{n_{2}}=0
.
As we have already shown,
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}\leq C
.
Observe
\tau_{g_{2}}\mathsf{df}_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}=\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}(1-\zeta_{2})\leq C
, where the equality uses the fixed point equation (
57
) and the inequality uses Lemma
C.1
.
Further,
\sqrt{n_{2}}\tau_{g_{2}}\zeta_{2}\rho_{g}^{\perp}>c
.
Because
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}

differs from
\sqrt{n_{2}}\tau_{g_{2}}\zeta_{2}\rho_{g}^{\perp}
by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
,
we conclude

\big\|\boldsymbol{A}_{2}\big\|_{2}<\frac{C^{\prime}\epsilon}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}.

(224)

Combining the bounds on
\boldsymbol{A}_{1}
and
\boldsymbol{A}_{2}
, we conclude

\big\|\nabla\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf})_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\big\|_{2}<\frac{C^{\prime}\epsilon}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}.

(225)

Third, we observe that by Eq. (
216
),

\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{u})
is
1/n_{2}
-strongly concave everywhere.

Step 4: we use these properties to establish upper bounds on the max-min problem.

Because

\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{u})
is
1/n_{2}
-strongly concave,
for any
\boldsymbol{u}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
with
\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}^{c}}=0
,

\displaystyle\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{u})

\displaystyle\leq\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf})+\nabla\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf})_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf})-\frac{1}{2n_{2}}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}\|_{2}^{2}

(226)

\displaystyle\leq\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf})+n_{2}\cdot\|\nabla\ell_{2|1}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf})_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{4n_{2}}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}\|_{2}^{2}.

Combining the previous display with Eqs. (
180
), (
212
), (
217
), (
220
), and (
225
),
we conclude that

\begin{gathered}\max_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\in E_{u|1}^{c}(\Delta)\\
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathcal{S}_{u|1}\end{subarray}}}\;\;\;\;\;\min_{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq C_{v}}\ell_{2|1}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\leq\ell_{2|1}^{*}+C^{\prime}\epsilon-\;\;\;\;\min_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\in E_{u|1}^{c}(\Delta)\\
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathcal{S}_{u|1}\end{subarray}}}\;\;\;\;\frac{\|\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}^{2}}{4n_{2}},\\
\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathcal{S}_{u|1}}\min_{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\leq C_{v}}\ell_{2|1}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\leq\ell_{2|1}^{*}+C^{\prime}\epsilon.\end{gathered}

(227)

By Eq. (
162
),

\Big|\phi_{u}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Big)-\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi_{u}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Big)\Big]\Big|\leq\frac{\Delta}{2}.

(228)

By the definition of
E_{u|1}^{c}(\Delta)
and because
\phi_{u}
is
1
-Lipschitz, on this event

\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{u}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}\in E_{u|1}^{c}(\Delta)\\
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathcal{S}_{u|1}\end{subarray}}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\|_{2}^{2}}{4n_{2}}\geq\frac{\Delta^{2}}{16}.

(229)

Combining Eq. (
227
) with the previous display and taking
\epsilon=\Delta^{2}/(32C^{\prime})
(with the same value
C^{\prime}
appearing in Eq. (
227
)),
we conclude the upper bound inside the probability in the first line of Eq. (
156
) holds.
The upper bound insdie the probability in the second line of Eq. (
227
) is given in Eq. (
227
).

In summary, we have shown that the upper bounds inside the probabilities in Eq. (
227
) hold on
\mathcal{G}_{2|1}(\epsilon,\sqrt{32C^{\prime}\epsilon})
for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
F.7
, we conclude that the probability bounds in Eq. (
227
) hold as well.

Appendix G
Concentration of empirical second moments

We will frequently need to use the concentration of second moment statistics of the form
\langle\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{a}^{(2)}\rangle
for random vectors
\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{a}^{(2)}
(possibly the same).
For example, this will be required in the proofs of Lemmas
E.9
and
F.7
, but also elsewhere.
Although
\langle\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{a}^{(2)}\rangle
is not globally Lipschitz,
its concentration will follow from Lipschitz concentration results along the lines of the marginal concentration (Lemma
E.1
) and conditional characterization (Lemma
D.3
) or standard Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions.
Some care is needed to track the correct dependence on
p
and
n_{k}
when extending these results to
\langle\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{a}^{(2)}\rangle
.
The following lemma provides the required extension.

Lemma G.1
.

Consider two random vectors
\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{a}^{(2)}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, constants
C,c>0
and
\mathsf{C}(\epsilon),\mathsf{c}(\epsilon):{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
,
p\geq 1
,
M,K>0
, and
\boldsymbol{S}\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{2}
, which satisfy the following concentration guarantee:

•

There exist
M
-Lipschitz functions
\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}

such that for Gaussian vectors
\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(2)}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\boldsymbol{S}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{n})
and any
1
-Lipschitz functions
\phi:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
,
\phi^{\prime}:({\mathbb{R}}^{n})^{2}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
,

\begin{gathered}\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\phi(\boldsymbol{a}^{(k)})-\mathbb{E}\big[\phi\big(\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(k)})\big)\big]\Big|>C\epsilon\Big)\leq\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}\;\;\text{for $k=1,2$},\\
\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\phi^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{a}^{(2)})-\mathbb{E}\big[\phi^{\prime}\big(\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(1)}),\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(2)})\big)\big]\Big|>K\epsilon\Big)\leq\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p},\end{gathered}

(230)

and

\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(i)}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(i)})\|_{2}^{2}]\leq C,\quad i=1,2.

(231)

•

The parameters above satisfy
M\leq CK/\sqrt{p}
and the singular values of
\boldsymbol{S}_{\xi}
are bounded by
C
.

Then there exist
C^{\prime},c^{\prime}>0
and
\mathsf{C}^{\prime}(\epsilon),\mathsf{c}^{\prime}(\epsilon):{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
depending only on
C,c>0
and
\mathsf{C}(\epsilon),\mathsf{c}(\epsilon):{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}

such that for any
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
we have

\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\langle\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{a}^{(2)}\rangle-\mathbb{E}\big[\big\langle\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(1)}),\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(2)})\big\rangle\big]\Big|>K\epsilon\Big)\leq\mathsf{C}^{\prime}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}^{\prime}(\epsilon)p}.

(232)

In all applications of Lemma
G.1
, we will take
C,c,\mathsf{C}(\epsilon),\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)
to be
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{regr}},\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}
, and regression-method dependent constants,
and
K,M
to be constants which possibly depend also on
p/n_{k}
.

Proof of Lemma
G.1
.

The function
\langle\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{a}^{(2)}\rangle
is
2C
-Lipschitz on
\{\|\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)}\|_{2},\|\boldsymbol{a}^{(2)}\|_{2}\leq 2C\}
.
Define
\overline{\boldsymbol{t}}(\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{a}^{(2)})
to be a
2C
-Lipschitz extension of
\langle\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{a}^{(2)}\rangle
which agrees with
\langle\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{a}^{(2)}\rangle
on
\{\|\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)}\|_{2},\|\boldsymbol{a}^{(2)}\|_{2}\leq 2C\}
.
By the first line of Eq. (
230
),
we have with probability at least
1-2Ce^{-cp}
that

\max\{\|\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)}\|_{2},\|\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)}\|_{2}\}\leq 2C
.
Thus, by the second line of Eq. (
230
), with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}-2Ce^{-cp}

\|\langle\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{a}^{(2)}\rangle-\mathbb{E}[\overline{\boldsymbol{t}}(\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)})]\|_{\mathsf{F}}=\|\langle\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{a}^{(2)}\rangle-\mathbb{E}[\overline{\boldsymbol{t}}(\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)})]\|_{\mathsf{F}}\leq 2CK\epsilon.

(233)

Moreover, becuase
\overline{\boldsymbol{t}}(0,0)=0
and is
2C
-Lipschitz,
we have
\big|\overline{\boldsymbol{t}}(\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)})\big|\leq 2C(\|\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)}\|_{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)}\|_{2})
.
By Cauchy-Schwartz, we have
|\langle\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)}\rangle|\leq\|\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)}\|_{2}
.
Thus

\big|\overline{\boldsymbol{t}}(\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)})-\langle\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)}\rangle\big|\leq\big(2C(\|\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)}\|_{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)}\|_{2})+\|\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)}\|_{2}\big)\mathbf{1}\{\max\{\|\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)}\|_{2},\|\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)}\|_{2}\}>2C\}.

(234)

By sub-Gaussian concentration, using Eq. (
231
) and that the singular values of
\boldsymbol{S}
are bounded above by
C
,
we have

\mathbb{P}(\|\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(i)}\|_{2}>2C+t)\leq e^{-(C+t)^{2}/(2CM^{2})}
,
whence

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\big[\overline{\boldsymbol{t}}(\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)})]-\mathbb{E}\big[\langle\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)}\rangle\big]\big|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}(4C(C+t)+(C+t)^{2})e^{-(C+t)^{2}/(CM^{2})}\mathrm{d}t\leq C^{\prime}M

(235)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}C^{\prime}(1+(t/M)^{2})e^{-\big(2(t/M)+(t/M)^{2}\big)/C}\mathrm{d}t\leq C^{\prime}M.

where
C^{\prime}
depends only on
C
and changes at each appearance, and in the second inequality we have used
M\leq C
to conclude that
(4C(C+t)+(C+t)^{2})e^{-C/M^{2}}\leq C^{\prime}(1+(t/M)^{2})
and
-2t/M^{2}\leq-2t/(CM)
.
We conclude that

\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\langle\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{a}^{(2)}\rangle-\mathbb{E}\big[\big\langle\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(1)}),\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(2)})\big\rangle\big]\Big|>C^{\prime}M+K\epsilon\Big)\leq\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}+2Ce^{-cp},

(236)

For
K\epsilon\leq C^{\prime}M
, we have
e^{1-C^{2}p\epsilon^{2}/(C^{\prime}(M/K)\sqrt{p})^{2}}>1
,
whence

\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\langle\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{a}^{(2)}\rangle-\mathbb{E}\big[\big\langle\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(1)}),\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(2)})\big\rangle\big]\Big|>2K\epsilon\Big)\leq\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}+2Ce^{-cp}+e^{1-(K/M)^{2}\epsilon^{2}/C^{\prime 2}},

(237)

because the bound becomes trivial for
K\epsilon\leq C^{\prime}M
and is implied by the previous display for
K\epsilon>C^{\prime}M
.
Because
(K/M)^{2}\geq p/C^{2}
,
the result follows.
∎

Appendix H
Empirical degrees-of-freedom: proof of Theorem
6.2
(iii) and Lemma
E.1
(ii)

We extend Theorem
6.2
(i) and (ii) to the case that
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}
and
\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}
are computed using
\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}
in place of
\mathsf{df}_{k}
(recall
\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}
defined in Eq. (
11
)),
and make the similar extension for Lemma
E.1
(i).
To distinguish between
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}
which are computed using
\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}
from those computed using
\mathsf{df}_{k}
,
in this section we denote by
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d},\mathrm{emp}},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d},\mathrm{emp}}

the fully empirical debiased estimates which use
\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}
.
As in the rest of the paper, the notation
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}
will denote the quantities computed using
\mathsf{df}_{k}
, as in Eqs. (
36
) and (
37
).

Our main tool is the following.

Lemma H.1
.

Assume
A1
and
A2
.

1.

There exist
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}
,
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{regr}}
and regression method-dependent
c^{\prime}>0
and
\mathsf{C},\mathsf{c}:{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
such that
for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}

\begin{gathered}\Big|\frac{\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}}{p}-\frac{\mathsf{df}_{k}}{p}\Big|<\epsilon.\end{gathered}

(238)

2.

There exist
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}
,
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{regr}}
and regression method-dependent
c^{\prime}>0
and
\mathsf{C},\mathsf{c}:{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
such that
for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}

\begin{gathered}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d},\mathrm{emp}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}\|_{2}<\frac{p}{n_{k}}\,\epsilon,\qquad\qquad\frac{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{l}}^{\mathrm{d},\mathrm{emp}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{l}}^{\mathrm{d},\mathrm{emp}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{l}}}\leq\frac{p}{n_{k}}\,\epsilon.\end{gathered}

(239)

Proof of Lemma
H.1
(i).

In the case of least-squares,
\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}=p=\mathsf{df}_{k}
, and there is nothing to prove.

In the case of the Lasso, this follows from Theorem 8 of
[
CMW20
]
,
where we recall that for the lasso we assume in
A2
that
c<n_{k}/p<C
, so that
n
and
p
may be interchanged at the cost of changing constants.

For ridge-regression, the bound follows from Theorem 3.7 of
[
KY17
]
, as we now describe.
By Eq. (
11
),
we have that
\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}/p=1-\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{k}}}\,\lambda_{k}\operatorname{Tr}\big(\frac{1}{p}\big(\frac{1}{n_{k}}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}+\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{k}}}\,\lambda_{k}\mathbf{I}_{p}\big)^{-1}\big)
.
The trace on the right-hand side is the resolvent of
{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}/n_{k}
evaluated at
-\lambda_{k}\sqrt{p/n_{k}}
and normlized by
1/p
.
Theorem 3.7 of
[
KY17
]
implies that this resolvent has fluctations
O_{p}(1/\sqrt{n_{k}})
from a deterministic quantity defined by Eqs. (3.5) and (2.1) of that paper, where
O_{p}
hides only
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{regr}}
and
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}
-dependent constants.
We compare these equations to the second line of the fixed-point equations Eqs. (
57
), which in the case of ridge regression do not depend on
\tau
.
Some algebra shows that the deterministic quantity given by
[
KY17
]
corresponds to
\zeta_{k}/(\sqrt{p/n_{k}}\,\lambda_{k})
.
Thus, we conclude that
\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}/p
has fluctuations
O_{p}(\sqrt{p}/n_{k})=O_{p}(1/\sqrt{p})
around
\mathsf{df}_{k}
.

We now show that the concentration occurs with exponentially (in
p
) high probability.
By Weyl’s inequality, the singular values of
{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}/\sqrt{n_{k}}
are
1
-Lipschitz in
{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}/\sqrt{n_{k}}
with respect to Frobenius norm.
Because
x\mapsto(x^{2}+\lambda_{k}\sqrt{p/n_{k}})^{-1}
is
C(n_{k}/p)^{3/4}
-Lipschitz,
we see that
\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}/p
is
C\sqrt{p/n_{k}}\lambda_{k}\,(n_{k}/p)^{3/4}\leq C(n_{k}/p)^{1/4}\leq C\sqrt{n_{k}/p}
-Lipschitz in the
{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}/\sqrt{n_{k}}
with respect to Frobenius norm.
By Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions,
we conclude that with probability at least
1-Ce^{-cp\epsilon^{2}}
we have
\big|\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}/p-\mathbb{E}[\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}/p]\big|<\epsilon
.
Combined with the fluctuation bound in the previous paragraph, we conclude that
\big|\mathbb{E}[\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}/p]-\mathsf{df}_{k}/p\big|\leq C/\sqrt{p}
.
By adjusting constants so that the bound becomes trivial for
\epsilon\leq C/\sqrt{p}
,
we conclude Lemma
H.1
in the case of ridge regression.
∎

Proof of Lemma
H.1
(ii).

Note

\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d},\mathrm{emp}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}\|_{2}\leq\frac{\|{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}\frac{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}\Big|\frac{1}{1-\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}/n_{k}}-\frac{1}{1-\mathsf{df}_{k}/n_{k}}\Big|.

(240)

By Eq. (
37
) and because
\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}=0
,

\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d},\mathrm{emp}}
and
\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}
differ only on the indices in
\mathcal{I}_{k}
.
Moreover,
one can write
\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\mathrm{d}}=\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}/(1-\mathsf{df}_{k}/n_{k})

and
\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\mathrm{d},\mathrm{emp}}=\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}/(1-\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}/n_{k})
.
Thus,

\frac{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{d},\mathrm{emp}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{d},\mathrm{emp}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}=\frac{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{d},\mathrm{emp}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{d},\mathrm{emp}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\leq\frac{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Big|\frac{1}{1-\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}/n_{k}}-\frac{1}{1-\mathsf{df}_{k}/n_{k}}\Big|.

(241)

where we have used in the inequality that
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}\leq\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}
.

We have that for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}
,

\|{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}/\sqrt{n_{k}}<C
and
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{k}}<C
,
where in the first bound we use

[
Ver10
, Corollary 5.35]
and that the singular values of
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
are bouned below and above by
c
and
C
,
and in the second bound we use the marginal characterization (Lemma
E.1
) and that
