\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{f}\|_{2}]/\sqrt{n_{k}}\leq\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]^{1/2}/\sqrt{n_{k}}\leq C
.
By Lemma
H.1
and using that
1-\mathsf{df}_{k}/n_{k}=\zeta_{k}>c
by Eq. (
57
) and Lemma
C.1
,
we conclude that for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}
,
\big|(1-\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}/n_{k})^{-1}-(1-\mathsf{df}_{k}/n_{k})^{-1}\big|<(p/n_{k})\epsilon
.
We conclude Lemma
H.1
(ii) by combining these bounds with the previous two displays.
∎

Using Lemma
H.1
, Theorem
6.2
(iii) follows from Theorem
6.2
(i) and (ii) and Lemma
E.1
(ii) follows from Lemma
E.1
(i).

Appendix I
The good marginal characterization event: proof of Lemma
E.9

Proof of Lemma
E.9
(Good marginal characterization event).

First, consider the concentration of
\boldsymbol{T}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}\Big)
.
For the first diagonal entry, there is nothing to show because it is a function of
\boldsymbol{e}_{k}
.
The conditional expectation of the second diagonal entry is
1
for all realization of
\boldsymbol{e}_{k}
, because
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}
is independent of
\boldsymbol{e}_{k}
.
Then,
Because
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{k}}
is a Gaussian vector with variance
\mathbf{I}_{n_{k}}
,
by Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions Eq. (
230
) is satisfied with
\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)}=\boldsymbol{a}^{(2)}=\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{k}}/\sqrt{n_{k}}
,
\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(2)}=\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{k}}
,
\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{a})=\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{a})=\boldsymbol{a}/\sqrt{n_{k}}
, and
K=\sqrt{p/n_{k}}
.
We see that
M=1/\sqrt{n_{k}}\leq C/\sqrt{p}
and
\boldsymbol{S}_{\xi}
has bounded singular values.
Moreover,
\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|_{2}^{2}]/n_{k}=1
, so Eq. (
231
) is satisfied.
Thus, Lemma
G.1
implies the desired concentration of the second diagonal entry.

Now consider the off-diagonal entries.
Because
\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}
is a Gaussian vector with variance
\tau_{e_{k}}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{n_{k}}
, and
\tau_{e_{k}}^{2}\leq C
,
by Gaussian concentration Eq. (
230
) is satisfied with
\boldsymbol{a}^{(1)}=\boldsymbol{a}^{(2)}=\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}/\sqrt{n_{k}}
,
\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(2)}=\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}
,
\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(1)}(\boldsymbol{a})=\boldsymbol{\phi}^{(2)}(\boldsymbol{a})=\boldsymbol{a}/\sqrt{n_{k}}
, and
K=\sqrt{p/n_{k}}
.
We see that
M=1/\sqrt{n_{k}}\leq C/\sqrt{p}
and
\boldsymbol{S}_{\xi}
has bounded singular values.
Moreover,
\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|_{2}^{2}]/n_{k}=\tau_{e_{k}}^{2}\leq C
, so Eq. (
231
) is satisfied.
Thus, we may apply Lemma
G.1
.
We conclude that for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}
,
\big|\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|_{2}^{2}/n_{k}-\tau_{e_{k}}^{2}\big|<\sqrt{p/n_{k}}\,\epsilon
.
Thus,
with probability at least
1-Ce^{-cp}
,
\|\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|_{2}^{2}/n_{k}\leq\tau_{e_{k}}^{2}+\sqrt{p/n_{k}}\leq C
, where the second inequality uses
A1
,
A2
.
For such
\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}
, the distribution of
\langle\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}},\xi_{h,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\rangle/n_{k}
conditional on
\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}
is Gaussian with variance
C^{2}/n_{k}
.
Then, the desired concentration of the off diagonal entries occurs by Gaussian concentration and the fact that
\|\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|_{2}^{2}/n_{k}\leq C
with high-probability.

Second, consider the concentration of
\|\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{k}}
.
The previous paragraphs shows that for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}
,
\big|\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|_{2}^{2}/n_{k}-\tau_{e_{k}}^{2}\big|<\sqrt{p/n_{k}}\,\epsilon
. Because by assumption
A1

\tau_{e_{k}}^{2}>c
,
taking square roots gives the desired concentration of
\|\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{k}}
.

Third, consider the concentration of
\boldsymbol{T}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{n_{k}}{p}}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f},\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}\Big)
.
By Eqs. (
81
), (
82
), and because proximal operators are
1
-Lispchitz
[
PB14
]
,
the vectors
\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}^{f}/\sqrt{p}
are
(\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\tau_{g_{k}}\vee 1/\sqrt{p})
-Lipschitz functions of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}
,
and by Lemma
C.1
, we have
\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\tau_{g_{k}}\leq C/\sqrt{p}
.
Thus, we may take
M=C/\sqrt{p}
in Lemma
G.1
.
By Gaussian concentration, we get Eq. (
230
) for
K=1
.
Finally, by Eq. (
320
), Eq. (
231
) is satisfied,
whence we may apply Lemma
G.1
and get the desired concentration.

Next, we establish the concentration of
\bar{\Omega}_{k}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f})
.
In the case of least-squares,
\bar{\Omega}_{k}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f})=0
always,
and there is nothing to show.
In the case of ridge-regression,
we apply Lemma
G.1
.
Recall that
\bar{\Omega}_{k}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f})=\sqrt{p/n_{k}}\,(\lambda/2)\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}
.
By Eqs. (
81
) and (
82
), because proximal operators are 1-Lipschitz
[
PB14
]
and
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
has singular values bounded below by
c
and
\tau_{g_{k}}\leq C/\sqrt{n_{k}}
by Lemma
C.1
,
we have that
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f}
is
M=1/\sqrt{n_{k}}
-Lipschitz in
\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}/\tau_{g_{k}}
, which is standard Gaussian.
Moreover, by the marginal characterization (Lemma
E.1
),
Eq. (
230
) holds for
K=\sqrt{p/n_{k}}
.
By
A2
and Lemma
C.1
, we have
\mathbb{E}[\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq 2\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}+2C\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq C(2+p/n_{k})\leq C
.
Thus, using that
\omega_{k}=\mathbb{E}[\bar{\Omega}_{k}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f})]
(see Eq. (
93
)),
we my apply Lemma
G.1
to get the required concentration of
\bar{\Omega}_{k}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f})
, where we use additionally that
\lambda<C
by
A2
.
In the case of the
\alpha
-smoothed Lasso,
we have that
\bar{\Omega}_{k}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f})=\lambda\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f}\|_{1}/\sqrt{n_{k}}
is

C\lambda\tau_{g_{k}}\sqrt{p/n_{1}}\leq C\sqrt{p}/n_{k}
-Lipschitz in
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}
.
The required concentration of
\bar{\Omega}_{k}(\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f})
follows by Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions.

Because
\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f}
is
\tau_{g_{k}}\leq C/\sqrt{n_{k}}
-Lipschitz in
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}
,
Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions implies that
\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f}\in E_{v}(\Delta/2)
with the desired high-probability.
Because
\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{f}
is
\zeta_{k}\tau_{h_{k}}\leq C\sqrt{p/n_{k}}
-Lipschitz function of in
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}
conditional on
\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{2}
,
Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions implies that
\boldsymbol{u}_{k}^{f}\in E_{u}(\Delta/2)
with the desired high-probability.

This completes the proof of Lemma
E.9
.
∎

Appendix J
Applications of the marginal characterization

In this section, we prove several consequences of the marginal characterization (Lemma
E.1
).
In Section
J.2
, we prove that
\phi_{\theta|1}(\mathsf{Cond}_{1})
and
\phi_{e|1}(\mathsf{Cond}_{1})
concentrate (Lemma
D.2
).
In Section
J.3
,
we prove that the good conditional characterization event occurs with high probability (Lemma
F.7
).
The proof of Lemmas
D.2
and
F.7
will require some corollaries of the marginal characterization which are stated and proved in Section
J.1
.

J.1
Corollaries of the marginal characterization

In this section, we prove Lemma
D.1
and state and prove two corollaries of the marginal characterization which will be useful in proving Lemmas
D.2
and
F.7
and Theorem
6.4
.
These proofs rely on the concentration of certain random-design quantities, which are easily established using Lemma
E.1
.
For convenient reference, we collect these concentration statements in the next lemma.

Lemma J.1
.

Assume
A1
and
A2
.
Then for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}
,

\begin{gathered}\Big\|\boldsymbol{T}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{n_{k}}{p}}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{k},\,\sqrt{\frac{n_{k}}{p}}\,{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})\Big)-\mathbb{E}\Big[\boldsymbol{T}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{n_{k}}{p}}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f},\,\sqrt{\frac{n_{k}}{p}}\,\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}\Big)\Big]\Big\|_{\mathsf{F}}<C^{\prime}\epsilon,\end{gathered}

(242)

and

\begin{gathered}\Big\|\boldsymbol{T}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\,\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\,\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}\Big)-\mathbb{E}\Big[\boldsymbol{T}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\,\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\,\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{f}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}\Big)\Big]\Big\|_{\mathsf{F}}<C^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{k}}}\,\epsilon,\end{gathered}

(243)

where

\begin{gathered}\mathbb{E}\Big[\boldsymbol{T}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{n_{k}}{p}}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f},\,\sqrt{\frac{n_{k}}{p}}\,\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}\Big)\Big]=\begin{pmatrix}(n_{k}/p)\tau_{h_{k}}^{2}&(n_{k}/p)\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}\mathsf{df}_{k}\\[4.0pt]
(n_{k}/p)\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}\mathsf{df}_{k}&n_{k}\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}\end{pmatrix},\\
\mathbb{E}\Big[\boldsymbol{T}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\,\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\,\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{f}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}\Big)\Big]=\begin{pmatrix}\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}&\tau_{e_{1}}\tau_{e_{2}}\rho_{e}&\zeta_{k}\tau_{e_{1}}\tau_{e_{k}}\rho_{e}^{\mathbf{1}\{k=2\}}\\[4.0pt]
\tau_{e_{1}}\tau_{e_{2}}\rho_{e}&\tau_{e_{2}}^{2}&\zeta_{k}\tau_{e_{2}}\tau_{e_{k}}\rho_{e}^{\mathbf{1}\{k=1\}}\\[4.0pt]
\zeta_{k}\tau_{e_{1}}\tau_{e_{k}}\rho_{e}^{\mathbf{1}\{k=2\}}&\zeta_{k}\tau_{e_{2}}\tau_{e_{k}}\rho_{e}^{\mathbf{1}\{k=1\}}&n_{k}\zeta_{k}^{2}\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}\end{pmatrix},\end{gathered}

(244)

where we interpret
0^{0}=1
when
\rho_{e}=0
,
and the entries of both matrices are bounded by
C
.

The same holds if we replace
\mathsf{df}_{k}
by
\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}
in the definition of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}
in Eq. (
36
),
where
\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}
is defined by Eq. (
11
).

Proof of Lemma
J.1
.

Both Eq. (
242
) and (
243
) follows from Lemma
E.1
using Lemma
G.1
.
Indeed,
by Eqs. (
81
), (
82
), and because proximal operators are
1
-Lispchitz
[
PB14
]
,
the vectors
\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f},\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}
are
\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\tau_{g_{k}}
-Lipschitz functions of
\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}/\tau_{g_{k}}
,
and by Lemma
C.1
, we have
\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\tau_{g_{k}}\leq C/\sqrt{p}
.
Thus, we may take
M=C/\sqrt{p}
in Lemma
G.1
.
Further, by Eq. (
80
),
we have
\boldsymbol{v}_{k}={\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})
,
whence the marginal characterization (Lemma
E.1
) gives us Eq. (
230
) for the pair of vectors
\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{k},\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})
with
K=C
.
Finally, by Lemma
C.1
,
(n_{k}/p)\tau_{h_{k}}^{2}\leq C
and
n_{k}\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}\leq C
, whence Eq. (
231
) is satisfied.
Thus, Lemma
G.1
implies Eq. (
242
).

By Eqs. (
81
) and (
40
) and because
\zeta_{k}\leq 1
,
the vectors
\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}}/\sqrt{n_{k}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}/\sqrt{n_{k}}
, and
\boldsymbol{u}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{f}/\sqrt{n_{k}}
are
M=1/\sqrt{n_{k}}
-Lipschitz functions of Gaussian vectors with variance
\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}
,
\tau_{e_{2}}^{2}
, and
\tau_{e_{k}}^{2}+\tau_{h_{k}}^{2}
, respectively.
By Lemma
C.1
these variance are all bounded by
C
.
Further, by Eq. (
80
),
we have
\boldsymbol{u}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}=\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}
,
whence the marginal characterization (Lemma
E.1
) gives us Eq. (
230
) for the vectors
\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}}/\sqrt{n_{k}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}/\sqrt{n_{k}},\boldsymbol{u}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{f}/\sqrt{n_{k}}
with
K=\sqrt{p/n_{k}}
.
Finally, by Lemma
C.1
,
Eq. (
231
) is satisfied,
whence we may apply Lemma
G.1
to conclude Eq. (
243
).
∎

We now prove Lemma
D.1
.

Proof of Lemma
D.1
.

Recall that
\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{k},\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{k}
are defined in Eq. (
143
) as
\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{k}=\tau_{g_{k}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}
and
\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{k}=\tau_{h_{k}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h}
, where
\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g},\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h}
solve the system of equations (
137
) and (
138
).
(For notational simplicity, in most of the paper—including in Eqs. equations (
137
) and (
138
)—we considered conditioning on the first regression,
k=1
. This was arbitrary, and in the current proof we consider general
k
.)
Writing the solution to these equations explicitly gives

\begin{gathered}\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}=\frac{n_{k}\zeta_{k}\tau_{h_{k}}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\|_{2}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\big(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}\big)+\frac{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{y}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}-{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\|_{2}},\\[4.0pt]
\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h}:=\begin{pmatrix}-\frac{{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\|_{2}}+\frac{\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\|_{2}}({\boldsymbol{y}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}-{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})\\[3.0pt]
-\frac{{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\|_{2}}\end{pmatrix}.\end{gathered}

(245)

The first line follows from the second equation in Eq. (
137
) by plugging in
n_{k}\zeta_{k}\tau_{h_{k}}
for
\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h},\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\rangle
, recalling the definition of
\boldsymbol{s}_{1}
in Eq. (
136
), and rearranging.
The second line follows by rearranging the first equation in Eq. (
137
) and using the definition of
\boldsymbol{t}_{1}
in Eq. (
136
).

First consider
\big\|\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})\big\|_{2}
.
Recalling the definition of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}
in Eq. (
36
) and that
n_{k}-\mathsf{df}_{k}=n_{k}\zeta_{k}
by Eq. (
57
),
the first line of Eq. (
245
) and the triangle inequality imply

\big\|\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})\big\|_{2}\leq\Big|\frac{n_{k}\zeta_{k}\tau_{h_{k}}\tau_{g_{k}}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\|_{2}}-1\Big|\cdot\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}+\Big|\frac{n_{k}\tau_{g_{k}}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\|_{2}}-\frac{1}{\zeta_{k}}\Big|\frac{\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{y}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}-{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})\|_{2}}{n_{k}}.

(246)

Because
\boldsymbol{v}_{k}={\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})
(see Eq. (
81
)),
by Lemma
J.1

with probability at least
1-Ce^{-cp}
we have
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}\leq C\sqrt{p/n_{k}}
.
Further, by Lemma
J.1
,
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}

\Big|\frac{n_{k}\zeta_{k}\tau_{h_{k}}\tau_{g_{k}}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\|_{2}}-1\Big|=\Big|\frac{(\sqrt{n_{k}}\,\tau_{g_{k}}\zeta_{k})(\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\tau_{h_{k}})}{(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{k}})(\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\|_{2})}-1\Big|<C^{\prime}\epsilon,

(247)

where we use that
\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\|_{2}
and
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{k}}=\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{k}}
concentrate on
\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\tau_{h_{k}}
and
\sqrt{n_{k}}\,\tau_{g_{k}}\zeta_{k}
, which are bounded above and below by
C
and
c
.
Recalling that
\boldsymbol{u}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}={\boldsymbol{y}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}-{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}
and applying
[
Ver10
, Corollary 5.35]
and Lemma
J.1
,
we have with probability at least
1-Ce^{-cp}

\frac{\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{y}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}-{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})\|_{2}}{n_{k}}\leq\frac{\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}\frac{\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}-{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}\leq C^{\prime},

(248)

and with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}
,

\Big|\frac{n_{k}\tau_{g_{k}}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\|_{2}}-\frac{1}{\zeta_{k}}\Big|=\Big|\frac{\sqrt{n_{k}}\,\tau_{g_{k}}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{k}}}-\frac{1}{\zeta_{k}}\Big|<C^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{k}}}\,\epsilon,

(249)

where we use that
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{k}}
concentrates on
\sqrt{n_{k}}\,\tau_{g_{k}}
which is bounded above and bewlo by
C
and
c
.
Combining the above bounds gives the high-probability bound on
\big\|\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})\big\|_{2}
in Lemma
D.1
.

Next, consider
\big\|\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}-(\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}})\big\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{k}}
.
Recalling the definition of
\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}
in Eq. (
37
) and that
\mathsf{df}_{k}/(n_{k}-\mathsf{df}_{k})=(1-\zeta_{k})/\zeta_{k}
by Eq. (
57
),
the second line of Eq. (
245
) and the triangle inequality imply

\frac{\big\|\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}-(\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}})\big\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}\leq\Big|\frac{\tau_{h_{k}}}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\|_{2}}-1\Big|\cdot\frac{\|{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}+\Big|\frac{\tau_{h_{k}}\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\|_{2}}-\frac{1-\zeta_{k}}{\zeta_{k}}\Big|\cdot\frac{\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}-{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}.

(250)

By
[
Ver10
, Corollary 5.35]
and Lemma
J.1
,
with probability at least
1-Ce^{-cp}
we have
\|{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{k}}\leq\|{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}\|_{2}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}\leq C^{\prime}\sqrt{p/n_{k}}

and
\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}-{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{k}}=\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{k}}\leq C^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
J.1
,
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}
we have

\Big|\frac{\tau_{h_{k}}}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\|_{2}}-1\Big|=\Big|\frac{\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\tau_{h_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\|_{2}}-1\Big|<C^{\prime}\epsilon,

(251)

where we have used that
\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\|_{2}
concentrates on
\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\tau_{h_{k}}
,
which is bounded above and below by
C
and
c
.
By Lemma
J.1
and the bound on
\big\|\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})\big\|_{2}
in Lemma
D.1
(which we have already proved),
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\rangle}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}-\sqrt{n_{k}}\,\tau_{g_{k}}(1-\zeta_{k})\Big|=\frac{p}{n_{k}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{k}}\,\tau_{g_{k}}}\Big|\Big\langle\sqrt{\frac{n_{k}}{p}}\,\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{k},\sqrt{\frac{n_{k}}{p}}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\Big\rangle-\frac{n_{k}\,\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}\mathsf{df}_{k}}{p}\Big|

(252)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\|_{2}+C^{\prime}\frac{p}{n_{k}}\Big|\Big\langle\sqrt{\frac{n_{k}}{p}}\,{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}),\sqrt{\frac{n_{k}}{p}}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\Big\rangle-\frac{n_{k}\,\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}\mathsf{df}_{k}}{p}\Big|\leq C^{\prime}\frac{p}{n_{k}}\,\epsilon,

where in the first equality we have used that
n_{k}(1-\zeta_{k})=\mathsf{df}_{k}
by Eq. (
57
),
and in the first inequality we have used that
\sqrt{n_{k}}\tau_{g_{k}}\geq c
by Lemma
C.1
to replace it by a constant.
Combining the above bounds,
we have with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}

\Big|\frac{\tau_{h_{k}}\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\|_{2}}-\frac{1-\zeta_{k}}{\zeta_{k}}\Big|=\Big|\frac{(\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\rangle/\sqrt{n_{k}})(\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\tau_{h_{k}})}{(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{k}})(\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\|_{2})}-\frac{1-\zeta_{k}}{\zeta_{k}}\Big|<C^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{k}}}\,\epsilon,

(253)

where we use that
\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\|_{2}
concentrates on
\sqrt{n_{k}/p}\,\tau_{h_{k}}
,

\|\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{k}}
concentrates on
\sqrt{n_{k}}\,\tau_{g_{k}}\zeta_{k}
, which are bounded above and below by
C
and
c
,
and that
\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}\rangle/\sqrt{n_{k}}
concentrates on
n_{k}\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}\mathsf{df}_{k}/p
, which is bounded above by
C
by Lemma
C.1
.
Combining the above bounds gives the high probability bound on
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}-(\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}})\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{k}}
in Lemma
D.1
.
∎

To prove Lemmas
D.2
and
F.7
and Theorem
6.4
,
we must extend the marginal characterization.
In particular, we extend the characterization to the vectors
\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1}
and
\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1}
,
and extend the characterization from the index set
\mathcal{I}_{1}
to the index set
\mathcal{I}_{2}
.
The next corollaries
perform these extensions.

Corollary J.2
.

Assume
A1
and
A2
.

There exist
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}
,
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{regr}}
and regression method-dependent
c^{\prime}>0
and
\mathsf{C},\mathsf{c}:{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
such that
for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
and 1-Lipschitz
\phi_{\theta}:({\mathbb{R}}^{p})^{2}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
,
\phi_{e}:({\mathbb{R}}^{n_{k}})^{4}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
, with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}

\begin{gathered}\big|\phi_{\theta}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k},\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{k})-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\theta}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f},\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f})]\big|<\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{k}}}\,\epsilon,\\
\Big|\phi_{e}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}\Big)-\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi_{e}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{f}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{h}_{k}^{f}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}\Big)\Big]\Big|<\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{k}}}\,\epsilon.\end{gathered}

(254)

Proof of Corollary
J.2
.

By Lemma
D.1
,
for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}

\Big|\phi_{e}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}\Big)-\phi_{e}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}\Big)\Big|<\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{k}}}\,\epsilon.

(255)

Using the marginal characterization (Lemma
E.1
) and recalling that
\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{f,\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}=\boldsymbol{h}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{f}
(see Eqs. (
38
) and
(
40
)),
we have for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}

\Big|\phi_{e}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}\Big)-\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi_{e}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{f}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{h}_{k}^{f}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}\Big)\Big]\Big|<\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{k}}}\,\epsilon.

(256)

The corollary follows by combining the above two bounds using the triangle inequality.
The first line of the corollary follows similarly, using the bound on
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})\|_{2}
from Lemma
D.1
and recalling that in the fixed design model
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f,\mathrm{d}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})=\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}
.
∎

Corollary J.3
.

Assume
A1
and
A2
.

1.

Let
\phi_{e}:({\mathbb{R}}^{n_{2}})^{4}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
by
M_{e}
-Lipschitz in its first three arguments and
M_{h}
Lipschitz in its final argument.
There exist
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}}
,
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{regr}}
and regression method-dependent
c^{\prime}>0
and
\mathsf{C},\mathsf{c}:{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
such that
for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}

\begin{gathered}\Big|\phi_{e}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Big)-\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi_{e}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Big)\Big]\Big|<\Big(M_{e}\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{2}}}+M_{h}\sqrt{\frac{p(p+n_{12})}{n_{1}n_{2}}}\;\Big)\,\epsilon.\end{gathered}

(257)

2.

Further, we have for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}

\frac{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}-(\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}})\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{1}}}\,\epsilon.

(258)

Thus, if we replace
\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}
by
\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}
in Eq. (
257
),
the same bound holds except that we must replace the term
M_{h}\sqrt{p(p+n_{12})/(n_{1}n_{2})}
on the right-hand side by
M_{h}\sqrt{p/n_{1}}
.

3.

Part (ii) still holds if we replace
\mathsf{df}_{k}
by
\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}
in the definition of
\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1}^{\mathrm{d}}
in Eq. (
37
),
where
\hat{\mathsf{df}}_{k}
is defined by Eq. (
11
).

Proof of Corollary
J.3
.

Observe that the data
\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}}
is rotationally invariant in the sense that for any orthogonal matrix
\boldsymbol{Q}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{1}\times n_{1}}
(i.e.,
\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Q}=\mathbf{I}_{n_{k}}
),
we have

(\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{Q}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{d}}}{{=}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}},{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}})
.
By the rotational invariance of the least-squares loss,
under this replacement
(\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}})

is replaced with
(\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{Q}\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{Q}\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}})
and
\boldsymbol{v}_{k}
is unaffected.
We conclude that
(\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}})
has a rotationally invariant distribution.
Further, from Eq. (
245
) and recalling that
\boldsymbol{v}_{k}={\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})
(see Eq. (
80
)) and
{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}^{c}}
is independent of
{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}}
,
we have
\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{1}^{c}}=-{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}^{c}}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1})/\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{N-n_{1}})
independent of
\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}}
.
Therefore,
\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}^{c}}=\tau_{h_{1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{1}^{c}}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\tau_{h_{1}}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{N-n_{1}})
independent of
\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}}
.
Thus, we may represent

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Big)

(259)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{d}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\left(\begin{pmatrix}(\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}})_{\mathcal{I}_{1}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}}\\[3.0pt]
\tau_{e_{1}}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(1)}\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}(\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}})_{\mathcal{I}_{1}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}}\\[3.0pt]
\tau_{e_{2}}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(2)}\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}(\boldsymbol{Q}\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}})_{\mathcal{I}_{1}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}}\\[3.0pt]
\boldsymbol{0}\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}(\boldsymbol{Q}\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}})_{\mathcal{I}_{1}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}}\\[3.0pt]
\tau_{h_{1}}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(3)}\end{pmatrix}\right)=:\boldsymbol{D},

where
\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(2)},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(3)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{iid}}}{{\sim}}\mathsf{N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{n_{2}-n_{12}})
and
\boldsymbol{Q}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{1}\times n_{1}}
is drawn uniformly from the special orthogonal group
SO(n_{1})
and independently of everything else.
We introduce the notation
\boldsymbol{D}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{2}\times 4}
for notational compactness,
and denote
\phi_{e}
applied to its columns by
\phi_{e}(\boldsymbol{D})
.
By the distributional equivalence above,
it is enough to establish the concentration result for
\phi_{e}(\boldsymbol{D})
in place of

\phi_{e}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Big)
.

Conditioning on
\boldsymbol{Q},\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}
, the only randomness that remains is in
\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(2)},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(3)}
.
By Lemma
C.1
and assumption
A1
, we have
\tau_{e_{1}},\tau_{e_{2}}\leq C
and
\tau_{h_{1}}\leq C\sqrt{p/n_{1}}
.
Thus, by Gaussian concentration,
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}

\big|\phi_{e}(\boldsymbol{D})-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{e}(\boldsymbol{D})\mid\boldsymbol{Q},\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}]\big|<\Big(M_{e}\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{2}}}+M_{h}\frac{p}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}\;\Big)\epsilon.

(260)

Now note that
\mathbb{E}[\phi_{e}(\boldsymbol{D})\mid\boldsymbol{Q},\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}]
viewed as a function of
\boldsymbol{Q}
with
\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{2},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1},\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(2)},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(3)}
fixed is
M_{e}(\|\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}\|_{2}+\|\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}}\|_{2}+\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}\|_{2})/\sqrt{n_{2}}+M_{h}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
-Lipschitz in
\boldsymbol{Q}
in Frobenius norm.
Indeed,
\|\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}-\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}^{\prime}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}\leq\|\boldsymbol{Q}-\boldsymbol{Q}^{\prime}\|_{\mathsf{F}}\|\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
, and likewise for the other arguments.
By concentration on the special orthogonal group (see, e.g.,
[
Ver18
, Theorem 5.2.7]
),
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\phi_{e}(\boldsymbol{D})\mid\boldsymbol{Q},\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}]-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{e}(\boldsymbol{D})\mid\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}]\big|

(261)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{1}}}\frac{M_{e}(\|\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}\|_{2}+\|\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}}\|_{2}+\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}\|_{2})+M_{h}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\,\epsilon.

Using Lemma
J.1
and recalling that
\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}=\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}
(see Eq. (
80
)),
with probability at least
1-Ce^{-cp}

we have
\big(M_{e}(\|\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}\|_{2}+\|\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}}\|_{2}+\|\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}\|_{2})+M_{h}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}\|_{2}\big)/\sqrt{n_{2}}\leq C(M_{e}\sqrt{n_{1}/n_{2}}+M_{h}\sqrt{p/n_{2}})
.
Thus,
for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}

\big|\mathbb{E}[\phi_{e}(\boldsymbol{D})|\boldsymbol{Q},\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}]-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{e}(\boldsymbol{D})|\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}]\big|<\Big(M_{e}\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{2}}}+M_{h}\frac{p}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}\;\Big)\,\epsilon.

(262)

Now denote
\tilde{\phi}_{e}(\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}/\sqrt{n_{1}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}}/\sqrt{n_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}/\sqrt{n_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}/\sqrt{n_{1}}):=\mathbb{E}[\phi_{e}(\boldsymbol{D})|\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}]
.
Using the Lipschitz properties of
\phi_{e}
,
we have

\displaystyle\Big|\tilde{\phi}_{e}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}\Big)-\tilde{\phi}_{e}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}\Big)\Big|

(263)

\displaystyle\leq M_{e}\sqrt{\frac{n_{1}}{n_{2}}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{Q}}\big[\|(\boldsymbol{Q}(\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}^{\prime}))_{\mathcal{I}_{1}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}\big]}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}\leq M_{e}\sqrt{\frac{n_{1}}{n_{2}}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{Q}}\big[\|(\boldsymbol{Q}(\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}^{\prime}))_{\mathcal{I}_{1}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}^{2}\big]^{1/2}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}

\displaystyle=M_{e}\sqrt{\frac{n_{12}}{n_{2}}}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}^{\prime}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}},

where we have used that
\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{Q}}\big[\|(\boldsymbol{Q}(\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}^{\prime}))_{\mathcal{I}_{1}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}^{2}\big]=n_{12}\|\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}/n_{1}
by symmetry and the rotationally invariant distribution of
\boldsymbol{Q}
.
Thus,
\tilde{\phi}_{e}
is
M_{e}\sqrt{n_{12}/n_{2}}
-Lipschitz in
\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}/\sqrt{n_{1}}
.
Similarly, it is
M_{e}\sqrt{n_{12}/n_{2}}
-Lipschitz in
\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}}/\sqrt{n_{1}}
and
\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}/\sqrt{n_{1}}

and
M_{h}\sqrt{n_{12}/n_{2}}
-Lipschitz in
\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}/\sqrt{n_{1}}
.
In particular, it is
(M_{e}+M_{h})\sqrt{n_{12}/n_{2}}
-Lipschitz in all its arguments.
Recalling the definition of
\tilde{\phi}_{e}
,
Corollary
J.2
implies that for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}

\big|\mathbb{E}[\phi_{e}(\boldsymbol{D})|\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}]-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{e}(\boldsymbol{D})]\big|<(M_{e}+M_{h})\sqrt{\frac{n_{12}p}{n_{1}n_{2}}}\,\epsilon.

(264)

Combining Eqs. (
260
), (
262
), and (
264
) and using
n_{12}\leq n_{1}
gives Eq. (
257
),
and the proof of Corollary
J.3
(i) is complete.

Now we consider replacing
\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}
by
\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}
.
By Eq. (
257
), with probability at least
1-Ce^{-pc}
we have
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}\leq\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}\|_{2}]/\sqrt{n_{2}}+C\sqrt{p/n_{1}}\leq\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]^{1/2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}+\sqrt{p(p+n_{12})/(n_{1}n_{2})}\leq C\sqrt{p/n_{1}}
,
where in the second inequality we have used Jensen and that
p/n_{2}\leq C
and
n_{12}/n_{2}\leq 1
, and in the third inequality have used that
\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]^{1/2}=\tau_{h_{1}}\leq C\sqrt{p/n_{1}}
by Eq. (
61
) and Lemma
C.1
.
By Eq. (
37
)
we have
\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\setminus\mathcal{I}_{1}}^{\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\setminus\mathcal{I}_{1}}=-{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}\setminus\mathcal{I}_{1}}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1})
and by
Eq. (
245
)
we have
\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\setminus\mathcal{I}_{1}}=-(\tau_{h_{1}}/\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}){\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}\setminus\mathcal{I}_{1}}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1})
, whence for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}

\frac{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\setminus\mathcal{I}_{1}}-(\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\setminus\mathcal{I}_{1}}^{\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\setminus\mathcal{I}_{1}})\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}=\Big|1-\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}/p}\,\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{1}/p}\,\tau_{h_{1}}}\Big|\cdot\frac{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\setminus\mathcal{I}_{1}}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\leq C^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{1}}}\,\epsilon,

(265)

where the inequality uses Lemma
J.1
to bound
\big|1-(\sqrt{n_{1}/p}\,\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\|_{2})/(\sqrt{n_{1}/p}\,\tau_{h_{1}})\big|\leq C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
Further, by Eq. (
37
)
we have
\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\cap\mathcal{I}_{1}}^{\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\cap\mathcal{I}_{1}}=\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\cap\mathcal{I}_{1}}/\zeta_{1}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\cap\mathcal{I}_{1}}
,
whence by Eq. (
257
) we have for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}

\frac{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\cap\mathcal{I}_{1}}-(\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\cap\mathcal{I}_{1}}^{\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\cap\mathcal{I}_{1}})\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}=\frac{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\cap\mathcal{I}_{1}}-(\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\cap\mathcal{I}_{1}}/\zeta_{1}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\cap\mathcal{I}_{1}})\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\leq C^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{p(p+n_{12})}{n_{1}n_{2}}}\,\epsilon,

(266)

where we have used that in the fixed design model
\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\cap\mathcal{I}_{1}}^{f}=\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\cap\mathcal{I}_{1}}^{f}/\zeta_{1}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}\cap\mathcal{I}_{1}}
(see Eq. (
40
)).
Combining the previous two displays and using
\sqrt{p(p+n_{12})/(n_{1}n_{2})}\leq C\sqrt{p/n_{1}}
because
n_{12}\leq n_{2}
and
p\leq Cn_{2}
by
A2
, Corollary
J.3
(ii) follows.

Corollary
J.3
(iii) holds by applying Lemma
H.1
.
∎

J.2
Concentration of
\phi_{\theta|1}
and
\phi_{e|1}
: proof of Lemma
D.2

Proof of Lemma
D.2
.

Note
\phi_{\theta|1}
is a function of
\mathsf{Cond}_{1}
only via
\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1}
.
With some abuse of notation,
we will write
\phi_{\theta|1}(\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1})
for this function.
Similary,
\phi_{e|1}
is a function of
\mathsf{Cond}_{1}
only via
\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{2}
.
In fact, because the coordinates of the noise are independent in the fixed-design model,

\phi_{e|1}
is a function of
\mathsf{Cond}_{1}
only via
\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}},\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}
.
With some abuse of notation,
we will write
\phi_{e|1}(\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}},\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}})
for this function.

We claim that
\phi_{\theta|1}
is a
M_{1}+M_{2}(\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}/\tau_{g_{1}})\leq M_{1}+CM_{2}\sqrt{n_{1}/n_{2}}
-Lipschitz function of
\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1}
.
Indeed, by Eq. (
82
) and because proximal operators are 1-Lipschitz
[
PB14
]
,
we see that
\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f}
is a
1
-Lipschitz function of
\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}
.
Becuase
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f}={\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f}
(see Eq. (
81
)) and the singular values of
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
are bounded below,
we have that
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f}
is
C
-Lipschitz functions of
\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}
.
Because
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f,\mathrm{d}}={\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}+\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}
, we have
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{f,\mathrm{d}}
is a 1-Lipschitz function of
\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}
as well.
In the fixed design model, we may represent
\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{f}=(\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}/\tau_{g_{1}})\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f}+\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}
, where
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{p})
independent of
\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f}
.
Because the expectation conditional on
\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f}=\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1}
in Eq. (
67
) can be represented as an expectation over
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}
,
the above observations imply that

\phi_{\theta|1}(\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1})
is
M_{1}+M_{2}(\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}/\tau_{g_{1}})\leq M_{1}+CM_{2}\sqrt{n_{1}/n_{2}}
-Lipschitz in
\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1}
, where we have used Lemma
C.1
to bound
\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}/\tau_{g_{1}}\leq C\sqrt{n_{1}/n_{2}}
.
By the definition of
\phi_{\theta|1}
(see Eq. (
67
)),
we have
\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\theta|1}(\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f})]=\mathbb{E}\big[\phi_{\theta}\big(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f},\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f,\mathrm{d}}\big)\big]
.
Thus, by Corollary
J.2
,
for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}

\displaystyle\Big|\phi_{\theta|1}\big(\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1}\big)-\mathbb{E}\big[\phi_{\theta}\big(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f},\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f,\mathrm{d}}\big)\big]\Big|\leq\Big(M_{1}+M_{2}\sqrt{\frac{n_{1}}{n_{2}}}\Big)\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{1}}}\,\epsilon=\Big(M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{1}}}+M_{2}\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{2}}}\Big)\,\epsilon,

(267)

so that the first bound in Lemma
D.2
is established.

Similarly, we claim that
\phi_{e|1}
is a
C\sqrt{n_{1}/n_{2}}
-Lipschitz function of
\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}},\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
.
Indeed,
by Eq. (
40
),

\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k}^{f}=(\boldsymbol{e}_{k}+\boldsymbol{h}_{k}^{f})/\zeta_{k}
and
\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{f,\mathrm{d}}=\boldsymbol{e}_{k}+\boldsymbol{h}_{k}^{f}
are
C
-Lipschitz functions of
\boldsymbol{h}_{k}^{f}
because
1/\zeta_{k}\leq C
by Lemma
C.1
.
In the fixed design model, we may represent
\boldsymbol{h}_{2}^{f}=(\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}/\tau_{h_{1}})\boldsymbol{h}_{1}^{f}+\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}
, where
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{N})
independent of
\boldsymbol{h}_{1}^{f}
.
Because the expectation conditional on
\boldsymbol{h}_{1}^{f}=\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{2}
in Eq. (
67
) can be represented as an expectation over
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}
,
we see that
\phi_{e|1}(\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}},\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}})

is

M_{1}
-Lipschitz in
\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
,

M_{2}
-Lipschitz in
\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
,
and

M_{1}+M_{2}(\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}/\tau_{h_{1}})\leq M_{1}+CM_{2}\sqrt{n_{1}/n_{2}}
-Lipschitz in
\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
, where we have used Lemma
C.1
to bound
\tau_{h_{2}}/\tau_{h_{1}}\leq C\sqrt{n_{1}/n_{2}}
.
By the definition of
\phi_{e|1}
(see Eq. (
67
)),
we have
\mathbb{E}[\phi_{e|1}(\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}/\sqrt{n_{2}})]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi_{e}\Big(\Big\{\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Big\},\Big\{\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{d},f}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Big\}\Big)\Big]
.
By Corollary
J.3
,
for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}

\displaystyle\Big|\phi_{e|1}\Big(\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Big)-\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi_{e}\Big(\Big\{\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Big\},\Big\{\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{\mathrm{d},f}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Big\}\Big)\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle<\Bigg[(M_{1}+M_{2})\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{2}}}+\Big(M_{1}+M_{2}\sqrt{\frac{n_{1}}{n_{2}}}\Big)\sqrt{\frac{p(p+n_{12})}{n_{1}n_{2}}}\,\Bigg]\epsilon

(268)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(M_{1}+M_{2}\big)\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{2}}}\,\epsilon,

where we have used that
M_{1}\sqrt{p(p+n_{12})/(n_{1}n_{2})}\leq CM_{1}\sqrt{p/n_{2}}
because
n_{12}\leq n_{2}
and
p\leq Cn_{1}
,
and
M_{2}\sqrt{n_{1}/n_{2}}\,\sqrt{p(p+n_{12})/(n_{1}n_{2})}=M_{2}\sqrt{p(p+n_{12})/n_{2}^{2}}\leq CM_{2}\sqrt{p/n_{2}}
because
n_{12}\leq n_{2}
and
p\leq Cn_{2}
.
This complete the proof.
∎

J.3
The good conditional characterization event: proof of Lemma
F.7

Proof of Lemma
F.7
.

For many of the objects controlled on the event
\mathcal{G}_{2|1}(\epsilon,\Delta)
, our proof in fact shows a bettr rate (i.e., have a prefactor on
\epsilon
which is smaller in
p/n_{k}
).
We state the Lemma only with the rate that we need so as not to clutter notation.

First, we consider the concentration of

\boldsymbol{T}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\Big)
.
The required concentration follows from applying Lemma
G.1
to its arguments pairwise, as we now show.
Recall the definitions of
\boldsymbol{e}_{2},\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{f},\boldsymbol{h}_{1}^{f},\boldsymbol{h}_{2}^{f}
in Eqs. (
38
) and (
81
) and of
\tau_{h_{1}},\tau_{h_{2}},\rho_{h}
in Section
C.1
.
Using the bound
\zeta_{1}^{2}(\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}+\tau_{h_{1}}^{2})\leq C
by Lemma
C.1
,
we see that
\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{f}
is a
1
-Lipschitz function of a Gaussian vector with variance bounded by
C
, and by the definition of
\tau_{h_{1}},\tau_{h_{2}},\rho_{h}
, we see that
\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}/\tau_{h_{1}}
and
\boldsymbol{h}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}-(\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}/\tau_{h_{1}})/(\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}^{\perp})
are standard Gaussian.
Thus, the arguments to
\boldsymbol{T}
inside the expectation in the first line of Eq. (
162
) are
M=1/(\sqrt{n_{1}\vee n_{2}})\leq C/\sqrt{p}
-Lipschitz functions of a Gaussian vector with variance bounded by
C
.
Recall that
\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}=\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}
(see Eq. (
81
)) and
\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}=\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\tau_{h_{1}}
(see Eq. (
143
)), and
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}
is standard Gaussian independent of everything else.
Using that
\tau_{h_{1}}\geq c\sqrt{p/n_{1}}
by Lemma
C.1
,
we conclude that any function which is
1
-Lipschitz in
\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}},\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{1}},\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
is in fact
M_{e}=(1\vee\sqrt{n_{2}/n_{1}})
-Lipschitz in
\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
and
M_{h}=C\sqrt{n_{1}/p}
-Lipschitz in
\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
.
Then, Corollary
J.3
and Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions gives us Eq. (
230
) with
K=C(1\vee\sqrt{n_{1}/n_{2}})\sqrt{p/n_{2}}+C\sqrt{n_{1}/p}\,\sqrt{p(p+n_{12})/(n_{1}n_{2})}\leq C
, where we use that
p/n_{1}<C
,
p/n_{2}<C
and
n_{12}\leq n_{2}
.
By Eq. (
321
), Eq. (
231
) is satisfied,
whence we may apply Lemma
G.1
with
M=C/\sqrt{p}
and
K=C
to get the desired concentration.

Second, we consider the concentration of

\boldsymbol{T}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{n_{1}}{p}}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\sqrt{\frac{n_{2}}{p}}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf},\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}}{\sqrt{p}},\frac{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}}{\sqrt{p}}\Big).

As above, the required concentration follows from applying Lemma
G.1
to its arguments pairwise, as we now show.
Recall the definitions of
\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{f},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{f},\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{f}
in Eqs. (
81
), (
82
)
and of
\tau_{g_{1}},\tau_{g_{2}},\rho_{g}
in Section
C.1
.
Using the bound
\tau_{g_{k}}\leq C/\sqrt{n_{k}}
by Lemma
C.1
,
we see that
\sqrt{n_{1}/p}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{f}
,
\sqrt{n_{2}/p}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{f}
is a
1
-Lipschitz function of a Gaussian vector with variance bounded by
C
, and by the definition of
\tau_{g_{1}},\tau_{g_{2}},\rho_{g}
, we see that
\boldsymbol{g}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}/\tau_{g_{1}}
and
\boldsymbol{g}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}-(\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}/\tau_{g_{1}})/(\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}^{\perp})
are standard Gaussian.
Thus, the arguments to
\boldsymbol{T}
inside the expectation in the second line of Eq. (
162
) are
M=C/\sqrt{p}
-Lipschitz functions of a Gaussian vector with variance bounded by
C
.
By Eqs. (
143
), (
150
), (
151
), the bounds
\tau_{g_{2}}\leq C/\sqrt{n_{2}}
and
\tau_{g_{1}}\geq c/\sqrt{n_{1}}
,
and because proximal operators are 1-Lipschitz
[
PB14
]
,
the vectors
\sqrt{n_{2}/p}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf},\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}/\sqrt{p},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}/\sqrt{p}
are
C\sqrt{n_{1}/p}
-Lipschitz functions of
\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}/\sqrt{n_{1}}
;
and by Eq. (
87
) and because the singular values of
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
are bounded below by
c
,
we have
\sqrt{n_{1}/p}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{1}
is a
C\sqrt{n_{1}/p}
-Lipschitz function of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}
.
Then, Corollary
J.2
and Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions give us Eq. (
230
) with
K=C
.
By Eq. (
321
), Eq. (
231
) is satisfied,
whence we may apply Lemma
G.1
with
M=C/\sqrt{p}
and
K=C
to get the desired concentration.

Third, we consider the concentration of
\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})
.
In the case of least-squares,
\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})=0
always,
and there is nothing to show.
In the case of ridge-regression,
we apply Lemma
G.1
.
Recall that
\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{f})=\sqrt{p/n_{2}}\,(\lambda/2)\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}
.
By Eqs. (
81
) and (
82
), because proximal operators are 1-Lipschitz
[
PB14
]
and
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
has singular values bounded below by
c
and
\tau_{g_{2}}\leq C/\sqrt{n_{2}}
by Lemma
C.1
,
we have that
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{f}
is
M=1/\sqrt{n_{2}}
-Lipschitz in
\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{f}/\tau_{g_{2}}
, which is standard Gaussian.
Moreover, by the previous paragraph and because the singular values of
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
are bounded below by
c
, we have that
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{cf}={\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}+{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}

is a
C\sqrt{n_{1}/n_{2}}
-Lipschitz function of
\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}/\sqrt{n_{1}}
,
whence by Corollary
J.2
and Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions,
Eq. (
230
) holds for
K=\sqrt{p/n_{2}}
.
By
A2
and Lemma
C.1
, we have
\mathbb{E}[\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}+{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq 2\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}+2C\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq C(2+p/n_{2})\leq C
.
Thus, using that
\omega_{2}=\mathbb{E}[\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{f})]
(see Eq. (
93
)),
we my apply Lemma
G.1
to get the required concentration of
\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})
, where we use additionally that
\lambda<C
by
A2
.
In the case of the
\alpha
-smoothed Lasso,
we have that
\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})=\lambda\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}+{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\|_{1}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
is

C\lambda\sqrt{pn_{1}}/n_{2}
-Lipschitz in
\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}/\sqrt{n_{1}}
.
The required concentration of
\bar{\Omega}_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})
follows by Corollary
J.2
and Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions.

To get the required concentration of
\boldsymbol{u}_{1}
,
recall that
\boldsymbol{u}_{1}=\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1}
(see Eq. (
80
)).
Then we apply Lemma
E.1
to conclude that for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}
we have
\big|\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}-\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}\|_{2}]/\sqrt{n_{1}}\big|<\sqrt{p/n_{1}}\,\epsilon
and
\big|\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}^{c}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}-\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}^{c}}^{f}\|_{2}]/\sqrt{n_{1}}\big|<\sqrt{p/n_{1}}\,\epsilon
.
Then, because
\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}
is a Gaussian with variance bounded by
C
(see Eq. (
64
) and Lemma
C.1
) and
\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}^{c}}=0
,
we can use Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions to conclude that
\big|\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}\|_{2}]/\sqrt{n_{1}}-\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]^{1/2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}\big|<C/\sqrt{n_{1}}
and
\big|\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}^{c}}^{f}\|_{2}]/\sqrt{n_{1}}-\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}^{c}}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]^{1/2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}\big|<C
.
Thus, by adjusting
\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)
and
\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)
so that the bound becomes trivial with
\sqrt{p/n_{1}}\epsilon<C/\sqrt{n_{1}}
,
we conclude the required concentration of
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}
and
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}^{c}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{1}}
.

The required concentration of
\|\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
follows by Lemma
J.1
.
The required concentration of
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
and
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{2}}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
holds because
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}\|_{2}
and
\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{2}}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}\|_{2}
are nothing but the norm of univariate Gaussians.

Finally, we show
\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\in E_{v|1}(\Delta/2)
and
\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\in E_{u|1}(\Delta/2)

By the definition of the conditional fixed design model (see Eq. (
150
)),
we have
\mathbb{E}\big[\phi_{v}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{f})\bigm|\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f}=\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1}\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[\phi_{v}(\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf})\bigm|\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1}\big]
.
Moreover, the above discussion implies that conditional on
\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1}
,
\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}
is a
C/\sqrt{n_{2}}
-Lipschitz function of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}
. Thus, the required high-probability bound on
\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{cf}\in E_{v|1}(\Delta/2)
holds by Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions and because
p/n_{2}\leq C
by
A2
.
Similarly,
we have
\mathbb{E}\big[\phi_{u}(\boldsymbol{u}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}/\sqrt{n_{2}})\bigm|\boldsymbol{h}_{1}^{f}=\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1},\,\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{2}\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[\phi_{u}(\boldsymbol{u}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}/\sqrt{n_{2}})\bigm|\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1},\,\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{2}\big]
.
Further, conditional on
\hat{\boldsymbol{h}}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{2}
, we have
\boldsymbol{u}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{cf}/\sqrt{n_{2}}
is a
\tau_{h_{2}}/\sqrt{n_{2}}\leq C\sqrt{p}\,/n_{2}
-Lipschitz function of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}
, where the inequality follows from Lemma
C.1
.
Thus, we also conclude the required high-probability bound on
\boldsymbol{u}_{2}^{cf}\in E_{u|1}(\Delta/2)
by Gaussian concentration of Lipschitz functions.

The proof of Lemma
F.7
is complete.
∎

Appendix K
Proof of Theorem
6.4
: estimating noise covariance

We treat the on-diagonal and off-diagonal entries of
\boldsymbol{S}_{e}
separately.

On-diagonal estimation: noise variance.

In Theorem
6.4
,
k
indexes the set
\mathcal{I}_{k}
of samples used to perform the estimation,
but the theorem provides both an estimate of the noise for the first and second linear model.
We use
l
to index the linear model whose noise variance we are estimating.

Note that
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{l}^{\mathrm{d}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{l}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}=\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{l}^{\mathrm{d}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{l})-\boldsymbol{v}_{l}\|_{2}^{2}
,
were recall
\boldsymbol{v}_{l}={\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{l})

Applying Lemma
J.1
,

for

(269)

Off-diagonal estimation: noise covariance.

We establish the concentration of
\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}^{\mathrm{d}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1},\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{\mathrm{d}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}\rangle_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}=\langle{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}^{\mathrm{d}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}),{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{\mathrm{d}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2})\rangle

-Lipschitz in

and
\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{f}/\tau_{g_{2}}^{f}

.

with

.
Lemma
E.1
gives us the first line in Eq. (

) for vectors

.
7

7

See footnote
4

Further, as we computed above,
(n_{k}/p)\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f,\mathrm{d}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}]=n_{k}\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}(1-2\,\mathsf{df}_{k}/p)+(n_{k}/p)\tau_{h_{k}}^{2}<C

G.1
.
First, using that
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{f,\mathrm{d}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})=\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}
,
we compute the expectation

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\langle{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}^{f,\mathrm{d}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}^{f}),{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}^{f,\mathrm{d}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}^{f})\rangle]

(270)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f},\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{f}\rangle]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{f},{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1})\rangle]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f},{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2})\rangle]+\mathbb{E}[\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1},\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}\rangle_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}]

\displaystyle=\tau_{g_{1}}\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}(p-\mathsf{df}_{1}-\mathsf{df}_{2})+\tau_{h_{1}}\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}=S_{g,12}(p-\mathsf{df}_{1}-\mathsf{df}_{2})+S_{h,12},

61

\mathsf{df}_{k}
(see Eq. (

)).
Thus, Lemma

implies that for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least

,

\big|\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}^{\mathrm{d}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1},\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{\mathrm{d}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}\rangle_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}-S_{g,12}(p-\mathsf{df}_{1}-\mathsf{df}_{2})-S_{h,12}\big|<\sqrt{(n_{1}\wedge n_{2})/p}\sqrt{p^{2}/(n_{1}n_{2})}\,\epsilon=\sqrt{p/(n_{1}\vee n_{2})}\,\epsilon
.

We now study the concentration of
\widehat{S}_{\hat{e}^{d},12}^{(k)}
.
We decompose

\widehat{S}_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}},12}^{(k)}=\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\rangle}{n_{k}}+\frac{\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\rangle}{n_{k}}+\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\rangle}{n_{k}}+\frac{\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\rangle}{n_{k}}.

(271)

Because

are jointly Gaussian,
for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
, with probability at least

,
\big|\langle\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\rangle/n_{k}-S_{e,12}\big|\leq C\sqrt{p/n_{k}}\,\epsilon\leq C\sqrt{p/(n_{1}\wedge n_{2})}\,\epsilon
.
To establish the concentration of
\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}},\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\rangle/n_{k}

\epsilon<c^{\prime}

.
An equivalent argument, switching the roles of the indices

using Theorem

and Lemma

.
As justified in the previous paragraph,
we have that
\sqrt{n_{1}/p}\,(\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{f,\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1})/\sqrt{n_{k}}

are

-Lipschitz functions of Gaussian vectors with variance bounded by

.

with

In the case

,
Lemma

(in the case

(in the case

) for the vector

Likewise, Eq. (

Theorem

with

and

in the case

and
K=\sqrt{n_{1}/p}+\sqrt{n_{2}/p}(\sqrt{p/n_{1}}+p/n_{2})\leq C\sqrt{(n_{1}\vee n_{2})/p}
in the case

8
8

8

Note that we have used here the rate given by Theorem
6.2
because we want to accommodate the case that
\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}
is compute with

in place of

Finally, as we checked above
(n_{1}/p)\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]/n_{k}\leq C
and
