.
Thus, we may apply Lemma
G.1
.
Because
\mathbb{E}[\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{f},\boldsymbol{h}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{f}\rangle]/n_{k}=S_{h,12}
(see Eq. (
63
)),
we conclude that for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}
,

\big|\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{k}},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}^{\mathrm{d}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\rangle/n_{k}-S_{h,12}\big|\leq\sqrt{(n_{1}\vee n_{2})/p}\sqrt{p^{2}/(n_{1}n_{2})}\,\epsilon=\sqrt{p/(n_{1}\wedge n_{2})}\,\epsilon
.

Combining the concentration bounds on each term in the decomposition,
we have for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}
,
\big|\widehat{S}_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}},12}^{(k)}-S_{e,12}-S_{h,12}\big|=\big|\widehat{S}_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}},12}^{(k)}-S_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}},12}\big|<\sqrt{p/(n_{1}\wedge n_{2})}\,\epsilon
,
where we have used that
S_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}},12}=S_{e,12}+S_{h,12}
by Eq. (
63
).
Recalling that
\widehat{S}_{g,12}^{(k)}=(n_{12}/(n_{1}n_{2}))\widehat{S}_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}},12}^{(k)}
,
we conclude that for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}
,
(p-\mathsf{df}_{1}-\mathsf{df}_{2})\widehat{S}_{g,12}^{(k)}=(n_{12}/(n_{1}n_{2}))(p-\mathsf{df}_{1}-\mathsf{df}_{2})\widehat{S}_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}},12}^{(k)}
.
Using Lemma
C.1
,
we have that
(n_{12}/(n_{1}n_{2}))(p-\mathsf{df}_{1}-\mathsf{df}_{2})\leq C
.
Thus,
recalling that
(n_{12}/(n_{1}n_{2}))S_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}},12}=S_{g,12}
by Eq. (
42
),
the concentration bound for
\widehat{S}_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}},12}^{(k)}
implies that
for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}
,
\big|(p-\mathsf{df}_{1}-\mathsf{df}_{2})\widehat{S}_{g,12}^{(k)}-(p-\mathsf{df}_{1}-\mathsf{df}_{2})S_{g,12}\big|<\sqrt{p/(n_{1}\wedge n_{2})}\,\epsilon
.
Combining the above results and using that
S_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}},12}=S_{e,12}+S_{h,12}
, we conclude that

\Big|\widehat{S}_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}},12}^{(k)}+(p-\mathsf{df}_{1}-\mathsf{df}_{2})\widehat{S}_{g,12}^{(k)}-\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}^{\mathrm{d}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1},\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{\mathrm{d}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}\rangle_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}-S_{e,12}\Big|\leq\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{1}\wedge n_{2}}}\,\epsilon.

(272)

The proof of Theorem
6.4
is complete.
\square

Appendix L
Proof of Lemma
F.2
: the conditional Gordon inequality

The conditional Gordon inequality for regression (Lemma
F.2
) is derived by applying the marginal Gordon inequality (Lemma
E.5
) conditionally on
\mathsf{Cond}_{1}
, and then marginalizing over
\mathsf{Cond}_{1}
.
The key observation will be that conditioning on

\mathsf{Cond}_{1}

is equivalent to conditioning on a set of linear constraints on
\boldsymbol{A}
(recall we define
\boldsymbol{A}=-{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}
).
First, we develop a comparison inequality which holds when conditioning on a certain type of linear constraints on
\boldsymbol{A}
.

For any vectors
\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{e}_{2}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,
\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}

consider the event (over the randomness in
\boldsymbol{A}
)

\mathcal{E}_{2|1}:=\mathcal{E}_{2|1}(\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime}):=\Big\{\boldsymbol{A}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}+\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime}=0,\;\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}-\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime}=0\Big\}.

(273)

Consider the system of equations, repeated from Eq. (
137
) except now written for arbitrary
\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime}
(not necessarily those defined in Eq. (
136
)).

\begin{gathered}-\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}}\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\rangle+\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h}-\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime}=0,\qquad-\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}+\frac{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}}\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h},\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\rangle+\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime}=0.\end{gathered}

(274)

These equations have a solution
\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g},\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h}

if and only if
the equations (
273
) have a solution
\boldsymbol{A}
:

Lemma L.1
.

The following are equivalent properties of the quadruplet
(\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime})
.

1.

\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime}\rangle=0
.

2.

There exists
\boldsymbol{A}
such that the constraints (
273
) are satisfied.

3.

There exist
\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g},\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h}
such that the constraints (
137
) are satisfied.

For any
\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}
, under the additional constraint

\frac{\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h},\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\rangle}{n_{1}}=\alpha,

(275)

the solution
\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g},\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h}
to Eq. (
137
) is unique.

Lemma
L.1
is proved at the end of this section.
We are ready to state the conditional Gordon inequality, of which Lemma
F.2
is corollary.

Lemma L.2

(Conditional Gordon)
.

Fix
\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
and
\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}

such that
\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime}\rangle=0
.
Fix any
\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g},\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h}
satisfying Eq. (
137
) (which exist by Lemma
L.1
).

Let
\boldsymbol{A}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N\times p}
have entries
A_{ij}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{iid}}}{{\sim}}\mathsf{N}(0,1)
,

\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{iid}}}{{\sim}}\mathsf{N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{p})
, and
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{N})
, all independent,
and define

\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{cg}}(\boldsymbol{u}):=\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{u}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g},\qquad\boldsymbol{h}_{\mathrm{cg}}(\boldsymbol{v}):=\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h},

(276)

(where the subscript stands for “conditional Gordon”).
Define
\mathcal{E}_{2|1}=\mathcal{E}_{2|1}(\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime})
as in Eq. (
273
).
Then:

1.

If
E_{u}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,
E_{v}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
are compact sets,
then for any
t\in{\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v}}\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in E_{u}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{v}+\psi(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\leq t\bigm|\mathcal{E}_{2|1}\right)\leq 2\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v}}\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in E_{u}}-\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{cg}}(\boldsymbol{u}),\boldsymbol{v}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{\mathrm{cg}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\boldsymbol{u}\rangle+\psi(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\leq t\right).

(277)

2.

If
E_{u}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,
E_{v}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
are compact, convex sets,
then for any
t\in{\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v}}\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in E_{u}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{v}+\psi(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\geq t\bigm|\mathcal{E}_{2|1}\right)\leq 2\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v}}\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in E_{u}}-\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{cg}}(\boldsymbol{u}),\boldsymbol{v}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{\mathrm{cg}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\boldsymbol{u}\rangle+\psi(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\geq t\right).

(278)

We prove Lemma
L.2
at the end of this section.
First, we prove Lemma
F.2
as a straightforward consequence.

Proof of Lemma
F.2
.

Recall from Eq. (
136
) that

\boldsymbol{t}_{1}=\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{v}_{1}

and
\boldsymbol{s}_{1}=-\boldsymbol{A}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}_{1}
.
The KKT conditions for the min-max problem (
79
) state that
(\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{u}_{1})
is a saddle point for this min-max problem if and only if

\frac{1}{n_{1}}\boldsymbol{t}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}+\frac{1}{n_{1}}\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}-\frac{1}{n_{1}}\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}=0,\quad-\frac{1}{n_{1}}\boldsymbol{s}_{1}\in\partial\bar{\Omega}_{1}(\boldsymbol{v}_{1}),\quad\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}^{c}}=0.

(279)

Now consider any vectors
\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{e}_{2}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,
\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
satisfying
\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}^{c}}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{0}
,

\boldsymbol{t}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}^{\prime}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}^{\prime}
,
and
\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime}/n_{1}\in\partial\bar{\Omega}_{1}(\boldsymbol{v}_{1})
.
(The prime indicates that these will be treated as dummy variables, not as those variables defined via Eq. (
136
)).
The KKT conditions above imply that the event (over the randomness is
\boldsymbol{A},\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{2}
)

\mathcal{E}_{2|1}(\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime})\cap\Big\{\boldsymbol{e}_{1}=\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{\prime},\;\boldsymbol{e}_{2}=\boldsymbol{e}_{2}^{\prime}\Big\},

(280)

is equivalent to the event

\Big\{\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1}=\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime},\;{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1})=\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\;{\boldsymbol{X}}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1})=-\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime},\;{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1}=\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime},\;\boldsymbol{e}_{1}=\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{\prime},\;\boldsymbol{e}_{2}=\boldsymbol{e}_{2}^{\prime}\Big\}.

(281)

Thus,
the event that
\mathsf{Cond}_{1}=(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1},\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{X}}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}),{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{2})
obtains a certain value is equivalent to the event
\mathcal{E}_{2|1}(\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime})\cap\Big\{\boldsymbol{e}_{1}=\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{\prime},\;\boldsymbol{e}_{2}=\boldsymbol{e}_{2}^{\prime}\Big\}
for
\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{s}_{1},\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{t}_{1}
, and vice versa.
We can thus apply Lemma
L.2
conditionally on
\mathsf{Cond}_{1}
.

Take
\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{cg}}(\boldsymbol{u})
and
\boldsymbol{h}_{\mathrm{cg}}(\boldsymbol{v})
as defined in Lemma
F.2
.
Observe that
E_{u}=E_{u}(\mathsf{Cond}_{1})
is a deterministic set conditional on
\mathsf{Cond}_{1}
.
Thus, applying Lemma
L.2
(i) conditionally,
we have for any
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
that with probability 1,

\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v}}\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in E_{u}}L_{2}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\leq t\Bigm|\mathcal{E}_{2|1}(\mathsf{Cond}_{1})\right)\leq 2\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v}}\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in E_{u}}\ell_{2|1}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\leq t\Bigm|\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g},\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h}\right).

(282)

Taking the expectation of both sides of this inequality gives Lemma
F.2
(i).
Lemma
F.2
(ii) follows by the same argument using instead Lemma
L.2
(ii).
∎

We now prove Lemma
L.2
.

Proof of Lemma
L.2
.

Conditional on
\mathcal{E}_{2|1}(\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime})
,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{v}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{A}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}+\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{v}+\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{v}-\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{A}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{v}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{A}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}-\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}}-\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=:\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{A}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}+\phi(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{d}}}{{=}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{A}}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}+\phi(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}),

where
\tilde{\boldsymbol{A}}
is independent of and identically distributed to
\boldsymbol{A}
.
We emphasize that the distributional equivalence holds conditional on the event (
273
).
Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v}}\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in E_{u}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{v}+\psi(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\leq t\bigm|\mathcal{E}_{2|1}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v}}\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in E_{u}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{A}}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}+\phi(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})+\psi(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\leq t\right).

(283)

Let
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{p})
,
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{N})
.
By the marginal Gordon inequality (Lemma
E.5
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v}}\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in E_{u}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{A}}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}+\phi(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})+\psi(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\leq t\right)

(284)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v}}\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in E_{u}}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}^{\top}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}^{\top}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}+\phi(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})+\psi(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\leq t\right).

We now simplify the above expression.
Using Eq. (
137
),

\displaystyle\phi(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})

\displaystyle=\frac{\big\langle\boldsymbol{u},-\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\rangle/\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}+\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h}\big\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\rangle\big\langle-\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}+\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h},\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\rangle/\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2},\boldsymbol{v}\big\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}}

(285)

\displaystyle-\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\rangle\langle-\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}+\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h},\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\rangle/\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{u}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}}\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}\rangle+\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}}\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h},\boldsymbol{u}\rangle.

Substituting into Eq. (
284
),
we conclude

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v}}\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in E_{u}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{v}+\psi(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\leq t\bigm|\mathcal{E}_{2|1}\right)

(286)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\boldsymbol{v}\in E_{v}}\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in E_{u}}-\big(\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}+\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{u}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}\big)^{\top}\boldsymbol{v}+\big(\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}+\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{h}\big)^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}+\psi(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\leq t\right).

The argument when
E_{v},E_{u}
are also convex and
\psi
is convex-concave is equivalent but applies the convex version of the marginal Gordon inequality (Lemma
E.5
(ii)).
The proof is complete.
∎

Proof of Lemmas
L.1
and
F.1
.

We first prove Lemma
L.1
.
We write the system of equations (
137
) in matrix-form

\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{s}_{1}\\
\boldsymbol{t}_{1}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}\mathbf{I}_{p}&-\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime\top}/\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}\\
-\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime\top}/\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}&\|\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{2}\mathbf{I}_{N}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{g}\\
\boldsymbol{h}\end{pmatrix}=:\boldsymbol{M}\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{g}\\
\boldsymbol{h}\end{pmatrix}.

(287)

The matrix
\boldsymbol{M}
in the preceding display is rank
p+N-1
,
so that its column space has codimension 1.
Checking that
\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime\top}&\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime\top}\end{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{M}=0
,
we conclude Lemma
L.1
.

Lemma
F.1
follows from Lemma
L.1
taking
\alpha=\zeta_{1}\tau_{h_{1}}
and observing that by the KKT conditions, Eq. (
273
) is satisfied for
\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{u}_{1}
,
\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{s}_{1}
,
\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{v}_{1}
,
\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{t}_{1}
, and
\boldsymbol{A}=-{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}
.
∎

Remark L.3
.

The conditional Gordon inequality makes an interesting—though to us still mysterious—connection between the primal and auxilliary KKT conditions.
In particular,
Eqs. (
273
) are the KKT conditions for the regression optimization (
36
), and Eqs. (
274
) are the KKT conditions for the marginal auxilliary objective (
92
).
Indeed, the gradient of
-\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{mg}}(\boldsymbol{u}),\boldsymbol{v}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{h}_{\mathrm{mg}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\boldsymbol{u}\rangle
with respect to
\boldsymbol{u}
is
-(\boldsymbol{u}/\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2})\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g},\boldsymbol{v}\rangle+\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}
,
and its gradient with respect to
\boldsymbol{v}
is
-\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}+(\boldsymbol{v}/\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2})\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h},\boldsymbol{u}\rangle
.
Thus, for a quadruplet
(\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime})

for which
\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}^{c}}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{0}
,

\boldsymbol{t}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}^{\prime}-\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}}^{\prime}
,
and
\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime}/n_{1}\in\partial\bar{\Omega}_{1}(\boldsymbol{v}_{1})
,
Eq. (
273
) identifies those realizations of the random matrix
\boldsymbol{A}
for which the primal KKT conditions are satisfied at
(\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime})
,
and Eq. (
274
) identifies those realizations of the random noise in Gordon’s objective for which the auxilliary KKT conditions are satisfied at
(\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{s}_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{t}_{1}^{\prime})
.

Appendix M
Technical lemmas

M.1
Properties of fixed-point solutions: proofs of Lemmas
6.1
,
C.1
,
E.7
, and
F.5

Proof of Lemma
6.1
: existence of fixed-point parameters.

We first show that, for fixed
k
, the equations (
57
)
have a unique solution
\tau_{g_{k}},\zeta_{k}
(note that these equations do not depend on
\rho_{g}
or
\tau_{g_{l}},\zeta_{l}
for
l\neq k
).
We consider least squares, ridge regression, and the Lasso separately.

In the case of least squares, we may solve these two equations explicitly.
Indeed,
\mathsf{R}_{k}(\tau^{2},\zeta)=p\tau^{2}
and
\mathsf{df}_{k}(\tau^{2},\zeta)=p
,
whence by Eq. (
57
)

\text{for least squares,}\;\;\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}=\frac{\tau_{e_{k}}^{2}/n_{k}}{1-p/n_{k}}\;\;\text{and}\;\;\zeta_{k}=1-p/n_{k}\;\;\text{uniquely}.

(288)

Because
\tau_{e_{k}}^{2}>0
and
p<n_{k}
,
we have
\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}>0

In the case of ridge regression with
\lambda>0
,
we have the explicit form

\begin{gathered}\mathsf{R}_{k}(\tau^{2},\zeta)=\frac{p\lambda^{2}}{n_{k}\zeta^{2}}{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\Big({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{k}}}\,\frac{\lambda}{\zeta}\mathbf{I}_{p}\Big)^{-2}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}+\tau^{2}\operatorname{Tr}\Big({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}\Big({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{k}}}\,\frac{\lambda}{\zeta}\mathbf{I}_{p}\Big)^{-2}\Big),\\
\mathsf{df}_{k}(\tau^{2},\zeta)=\operatorname{Tr}\Big({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\Big({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{k}}}\,\frac{\lambda}{\zeta}\mathbf{I}_{p}\Big)^{-1}\Big).\end{gathered}

(289)

The degrees-of-freedom
\mathsf{df}_{k}(\tau^{2},\zeta)
does not depend on
\tau^{2}
, is continuous and strictly increasing in
\zeta
,
and is 0 when
\zeta=0
.
Thus, the second fixed-point equation (i.e.,
\zeta_{k}=1-\mathsf{df}_{k}(\tau_{g_{k}}^{2},\zeta_{k})/n_{k}
) has a unique solution
\zeta_{k}>0
.
Because
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\sqrt{p/n_{k}}\,(\lambda/\zeta_{k})\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}\preceq\mathbf{I}_{p}
and is diagonalizable,

\operatorname{Tr}\big({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\sqrt{p/n_{k}}\,(\lambda/\zeta_{k})\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-2}\big)\leq\mathsf{df}_{k}(\tau_{g_{2}}^{2},\zeta_{k})<n_{k}
.
Using the previous display and Eq. (
57
),
we may solve

\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}=\frac{\tau_{e_{k}}^{2}+p\lambda^{2}/(n\zeta_{k}^{2}){\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\sqrt{p/n_{k}}\,(\lambda/\zeta_{k})\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-2}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}}{n_{k}-\operatorname{Tr}\big({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\sqrt{p/n_{k}}\,(\lambda/\zeta_{k})\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-2}\big)}>0.

(290)

In the case of the Lasso and the
\alpha
-smoothed Lasso,
we apply results from the paper
[
CMW20
]
, which considers the same setting with a different covariate normalization.
In Section
M.4
,
we provide the change of variables to translate our normalization into theirs.
Under this change of variables,
the fixed point equations (
57
) are exactly equations (8a) and (8b) of
[
CMW20
]
.
Theorem 1 and Lemma A.2 of
[
CMW20
]
guarantee these equations have a unique solution when
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}

is invertible and
\tau_{e_{k}}^{2}>0
,
and the solution
\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}>0
.

Finally, we show that given a solution
\tau_{g_{1}},\zeta_{1},\tau_{g_{2}},\zeta_{2}
to Eqs. (
57
),
there is a solution
\rho_{g}
to the equation Eq. (
58
).
By Cauchy-Schwartz,
\mathsf{R}_{12}(\boldsymbol{S}_{g},\{\zeta_{k}\})\leq\sqrt{\mathsf{R}_{1}(\tau_{g_{1}}^{2},\zeta_{1})\mathsf{R}_{2}(\tau_{g_{2}}^{2},\zeta_{2})}
. Because also
n_{12}/\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}\leq 1
,
the right-hand side of the fixed point equation (
58
) is between

\pm\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}\sqrt{\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}\rho_{e}+\mathsf{R}_{1}(\tau_{g_{1}}^{2},\zeta_{1})}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\sqrt{\tau_{e_{2}}^{2}\rho_{e}+\mathsf{R}_{2}(\tau_{g_{2}}^{2},\zeta_{2})}=\pm\tau_{g_{1}}\tau_{g_{2}}\sqrt{\big(1-\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}\rho_{e}^{\perp 2}/(n_{1}\tau_{g_{1}}^{2})\big)\big(1-\tau_{e_{2}}^{2}\rho_{e}^{\perp 2}/(n_{2}\tau_{g_{2}}^{2})\big)},

(291)

where we have used the fixed point equations (
57
) to get the final equality.
Because
\tau_{e_{1}},\tau_{e_{2}},\rho_{e}^{\perp}>0
(see Lemma
C.1
),
we conclude the right-hand side of the equation (
58
) is always in the open interval
(-\tau_{g_{1}}\tau_{g_{2}},\tau_{g_{1}}\tau_{g_{2}})
.
Moroever, it is continuous in
\rho_{g}
.
Indeed,
in the definition of
\mathsf{R}_{12}
,
we may represent
\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{f}=(\tau_{g_{2}}/\tau_{g_{1}})\rho_{g}\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f}+\tau_{g_{2}}\sqrt{1-\rho_{g}^{2}}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}
where
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{iid}}}{{\sim}}\mathsf{N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{p})
independent of everything else.
Then continuity in
\rho_{g}
follows by dominated convergence using that

\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{f,\mathrm{d}}
and
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}
are
\tau_{g_{2}}
-Lipshitz in
\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{f}
(because proximal operators are
1
-Lipschitz
[
PB14
]
).
At
\rho_{g}=1
, the left-hand side of the fixed point equation (
57
) is
\tau_{g_{1}}\tau_{g_{2}}
, and at
\rho_{g}=-1
, the left-hand side is
-\tau_{g_{1}}\tau_{g_{2}}
.
The existence of a solution
\rho_{g}\in(-1,1)
follows by the intermediate value theorem.

We now show uniqueness of the solution
\rho_{g}
.
We denote by
\partial_{\rho_{g}}\mathsf{R}_{12}(\boldsymbol{S}_{g},\{\zeta_{k}\})
the partial derivative taken with
\tau_{g_{1}},\tau_{g_{2}},\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}
held fixed;
by
\nabla_{\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}}
the Jacobian with respect to
\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}
;
and for matrices
\boldsymbol{A},\boldsymbol{B}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times p}
by
\langle\boldsymbol{A},\boldsymbol{B}\rangle=\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{A}^{\top}\boldsymbol{B})
.
Then using the Dirichlet form for the Ornstein-Uhlenbeck process (see, for example, Sections 2.6 and 2.7 of
[
BGL13
]
),
we compute

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{\rho_{g}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{12}(\boldsymbol{S}_{g},\{\zeta_{k}\})\big|=\tau_{1}\tau_{2}\big|\mathbb{E}\big[\big\langle\nabla_{\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\eta_{1}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}+\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f};\zeta_{1}),\nabla_{\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{f}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\eta_{2}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}+\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{f};\zeta_{2})\big\rangle\big]\big|

(292)

\displaystyle\leq\tau_{g_{1}}\tau_{g_{2}}\mathbb{E}\big[\operatorname{Tr}\big((\nabla_{\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\eta_{1}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}+\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f};\zeta_{1}))^{2}\big)\big]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\big[\operatorname{Tr}\big((\nabla_{\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{f}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\eta_{2}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}+\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{f};\zeta_{2}))^{2}\big)\big]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\tau_{g_{1}}\tau_{g_{2}}\sqrt{\mathsf{df}_{1}(\tau_{g_{1}}^{2},\zeta_{1})\mathsf{df}_{2}(\tau_{g_{2}}^{2},\zeta_{2})}<\tau_{g_{1}}\tau_{g_{2}}\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}},

where in the second-to-last inequality we have used that

\nabla_{\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\eta_{1}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}+\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f};\zeta_{1})
is symmetric (because
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\eta_{1}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}+\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f};\zeta_{1})
is the solution to an optimization problem), and because
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\eta_{k}
is
1
-Lipschitz, its eigenvalues are bounded by 1.
Thus, the magnitude of the partial derivative with respect to
\rho_{g}
of the right-hand side of Eq. (
58
) is strictly less than
n_{12}/(n_{1}n_{2})\tau_{1}\tau_{2}\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}\leq\tau_{1}\tau_{2}
, where we have used that
n_{12}\leq n_{1}\wedge n_{2}\leq\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}
.
Because the partial derivative of the left-hand side of Eq. (
58
) with respect to
\rho_{g}
is
\tau_{1}\tau_{2}
,
we conclude the solution
\rho_{g}
is unique.
∎

To provide bounds on the fixed-point parameters in the proof of Lemma
C.1
,
we will need the following lemma.

Lemma M.1
.

Taking any
\tau_{g_{k}},\zeta_{k}
(not necessarily solutions to the fixed-point equations (
42
)),
there exists constant
C
depending only on
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{model}},\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{regr}}
such that when the
\alpha
-smoothed lasso is used in the fixed design model

\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}\leq\|\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}\|_{2}+\frac{C}{\zeta_{k}}\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{k}}}.

(293)

Moreover,

\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}]\geq\frac{c}{\zeta_{k}^{2}}\frac{p}{n_{k}}\Phi\Big(-\frac{C}{\sqrt{n_{k}}\,\tau_{k}\zeta_{k}}\Big),

(294)

where
\Phi(z)=\mathbb{P}(Z\leq z)
for
Z\sim\mathsf{N}(0,1)
is the Gaussian cdf.

Proof of Lemma
M.1
.

The KKT conditions for the optimization (
39
) are

{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})\in\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}-\frac{1}{\zeta_{k}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}\partial\Omega_{k}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}),

(295)

where for the Lasso and the
\alpha
-smoothed Lasso, we have
\|\boldsymbol{d}\|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{p/n_{k}}
for any
\boldsymbol{d}\in\partial\Omega_{k}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})
and any
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}
.
The first result follows.

To derive the second result,
we write

{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})\in{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}-\frac{1}{\zeta_{k}}\partial\Omega_{k}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f}).

(296)

Note
[\partial\Omega_{k}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f})]_{j}\in[-\lambda/\sqrt{n_{k}},\lambda/\sqrt{n_{k}}]
,
whence
\big|[{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})]_{j}\big|\geq\big|[{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}]_{j}\big|-\lambda/(\sqrt{n_{k}}\,\zeta_{k})
.
Thus,

\mathbb{E}\big[[{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})]_{j}^{2}\big]\geq\mathbb{P}\big(\big|[{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}]_{j}\big|>2\lambda/(\sqrt{n_{k}}\zeta_{k})\big)\lambda^{2}/(n_{k}\zeta_{k}^{2})\geq c\Phi\big(-C/(\sqrt{n_{k}}\,\tau_{g_{k}}\zeta_{k})\big)/(n_{k}\zeta_{k}^{2})
.
Because
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}\geq c\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})\|_{2}^{2}=c\sum_{j=1}^{p}[{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})]_{j}^{2}
,
the result follows.
∎

Proof of Lemma
C.1
: bounds on fixed-point parameters.

By the first equation of (
57
),
we have
\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}\geq\tau_{e_{k}}^{2}/n_{k}\geq c/n_{k}
.
We next determine an upper bound on
\tau_{g_{k}}
and lower bound on
\zeta_{k}
.
We do so for least-squares, ridge regression, and the
\alpha
-smoothed Lasso separately (where
\alpha=0
is the Lasso).

For least squares,
by Eq. (
288
) we have
\zeta_{k}=1-p/n_{k}>c>0
.
Also by Eq. (
288
),
we have
\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}\leq(\tau_{e_{k}}^{2}/n_{k})/\zeta_{k}\leq C/n_{k}
.

For ridge regression, there are two cases.
In the first case,
n_{k}/p\geq c>1
.
Then we use
\mathsf{df}_{k}(\tau^{2},\zeta)\leq p
(see Eq. (
289
))
to get
\zeta_{k}=1-\mathsf{df}_{k}(\tau_{g_{k}}^{2},\zeta_{k})/n_{k}\geq 1-1/c>0
.
Note
n_{k}-\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\sqrt{p/n_{k}}(\lambda/\zeta_{k})\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-2})\geq n_{k}-\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\sqrt{p/n_{k}}(\lambda/\zeta_{k})\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-}1)=n_{k}\zeta_{k}>n_{k}c
.
Thus, by Eq. (
290
),

n_{k}\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}\leq\frac{\tau_{e_{k}}^{2}+(p/n_{k})(\lambda/\zeta_{k})^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\sqrt{p/n_{k}}(\lambda/\zeta_{k})\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{\zeta_{k}}\leq C.

In the second case,
\lambda\geq c>0
.
Then we use the second line of Eq. (
289
) to bound

\mathsf{df}_{k}(\tau^{2},\zeta_{k})\leq Cp\zeta_{k}
.
The second line of Eq. (
57
) implies
\zeta_{k}\geq 1-C(p/n_{k})\zeta_{k}\geq 1-C\zeta_{k}
,
whence
\zeta_{k}\geq 1/(1+C)
.
Using, as above that
n_{k}-\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\sqrt{p/n_{k}}(\lambda/\zeta_{k})\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-2})\geq n_{k}c
, we conclude that
n_{k}\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}\leq C
.

For the Lasso and the
\alpha
-smoothed Lasso,
an upper bound on
\tau_{g_{k}}
and lower bound on
\zeta_{k}
holds by Theorem 2 and Lemma A.3 of
[
CMW20
]
. See Section
M.4
for the change of normalization and notation connecting the current paper to
[
CMW20
]
.

We now derive bounds on
\tau_{h_{k}}
.
For least squares,
\tau_{h_{k}}^{2}=\mathsf{R}_{k}(\tau_{g_{k}}^{2},\zeta_{k})=p\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}
, whence
cp/n_{k}\leq\tau_{h_{k}}^{2}\leq Cp/n_{k}
by the bounds on
\tau_{g_{k}}
.
For ridge regression,
we upper bound

\displaystyle\tau_{h_{k}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{p\lambda^{2}}{n_{k}\zeta_{k}^{2}}{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\Big({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{k}}}\,\frac{\lambda}{\zeta_{k}}\mathbf{I}_{p}\Big)^{-2}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}+\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}\operatorname{Tr}\Big({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}\Big({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{k}}}\,\frac{\lambda}{\zeta_{k}}\mathbf{I}_{p}\Big)^{-2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{p}{n_{k}}\frac{\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\sqrt{p/n_{k}}(\lambda/\zeta_{k})\mathbf{I}_{p})}+\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}p\leq Cp/n_{k}.

For the lower bound, we use that
\operatorname{Tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\sqrt{p/n_{k}}(\lambda/\zeta_{k})\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-2})\geq p\sigma_{\min}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})^{2}(\sigma_{\min}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})^{2}+\sqrt{p/n_{k}}(\lambda/\zeta_{k}))\geq cp
,
whence, using that
\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}\geq c/n_{k}
, we have
\tau_{h_{k}}^{2}\geq cp/n_{k}
.
Eq. (
289
) gives

\tau_{h_{k}}^{2}\leq(p/n_{k})(\lambda/\zeta_{k})^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}/\sigma_{\min}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\sqrt{p/n_{k}}(\lambda/\zeta_{k})\mathbf{I}_{p})+\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}p\leq Cp/n_{k}
.
For the
\alpha
-smoothed Lasso,
we use that by Lemma
M.1
,
we have
\tau_{h_{k}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}]\leq 2\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]+(C/\zeta_{k})^{2}p/n_{k}\leq Cp/n_{k}
by the upper bound on
\tau_{g_{k}}
.
For the lower bound, we apply the second display in Lemma
M.1
, and used that
\zeta_{k}\leq 1
and
\sqrt{n_{k}}\,\tau_{g_{k}}\zeta_{k}\geq c
, which gives
\tau_{h_{k}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}]\geq cp/n_{k}
.

Recalling that
\tau_{\hat{e}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}}^{2}=\tau_{e_{k}}^{2}+\tau_{h_{k}}^{2}
(see Eq. (
63
)),
the above bounds imply
c<\tau_{\hat{e}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}}^{2}<C
.

Next we upper bound
|\rho_{e}|
. Using the change of variables given in Section
6
,
we have
\rho_{e}^{2}=\kappa^{2}\beta^{2}/(\kappa^{2}\beta^{2}+\sigma^{2})=1-\sigma^{2}/(\kappa^{2}\beta^{2}+\sigma^{2})\leq 1-c
using that
C>\sigma^{2}>c
and
\kappa^{2}<C
,
\beta^{2}<C
.
We now upper bound
|\rho_{g}|
and
|\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}|
.
By Eq. (
58
) and recalling the bounds on its right-hand side given by Eq. (
291
),
we have
|\rho_{g}|\leq 1-c
.
Further, by Eq. (
63
) and using
|\rho_{h}|\leq 1
, we have
|\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}|\leq(\tau_{e_{1}}\tau_{e_{2}}+\tau_{h_{1}}\tau_{h_{2}})/(\tau_{\hat{e}_{1}^{\mathrm{d}}}\tau_{\hat{e}_{2}^{\mathrm{d}}})-\tau_{e_{1}}\tau_{e_{2}}(1-\rho_{e})/(\tau_{\hat{e}_{1}^{\mathrm{d}}}\tau_{\hat{e}_{2}^{\mathrm{d}}})
.
By Cauchy-Schwartz we have
(\tau_{e_{1}}\tau_{e_{2}}+\tau_{h_{1}}\tau_{h_{2}})/(\tau_{\hat{e}_{1}^{\mathrm{d}}}\tau_{\hat{e}_{2}^{\mathrm{d}}})\leq 1
.
Further, because
\tau_{h_{k}}^{2}\leq Cp/n_{k}\leq C
, we have by Eq. (
63
) that
\tau_{\hat{e}_{k}^{\mathrm{d}}}^{2}\leq C
, whence
\tau_{e_{1}}\tau_{e_{2}}(1-\rho_{e})/(\tau_{\hat{e}_{1}^{\mathrm{d}}}\tau_{\hat{e}_{2}^{\mathrm{d}}})\geq c
, where we have used Lemma
C.1
.
We conclude that
|\rho_{\hat{e}^{\mathrm{d}}}|\leq 1-c
.

We now upper bound
|\rho_{h}|
.
We may write

\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{f}=(\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}/\tau_{g_{1}})\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f}+\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}
for
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{p})
independent of everything else.
Define
\tilde{\boldsymbol{g}}_{2}^{f}=(\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}/\tau_{g_{1}})\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f}+\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}^{\perp}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}
, where
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{p})
independent of everything else.
Define
\tilde{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{2}^{f}
as we defined
\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{f}
except with
\tilde{\boldsymbol{g}}_{2}^{f}
.
We first claim that

\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{f}-\tilde{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{2}^{f}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}]\geq cp/n_{k}.

(297)

For ridge-regression (possibly with
\lambda=0
for least-squares),
observe that
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{f}-\tilde{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{2}^{f}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}=\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\sqrt{p/n_{k}}\,(\lambda/\zeta_{k})\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}(\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{g}}_{k}^{f})\|_{2}\geq c\|\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{g}}_{k}^{f}\|_{2}
,
where we have used that the singular values of
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
are bounded below and
\sqrt{p/n_{k}}(\lambda/\zeta_{k})\leq C
.
Then
\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{f}-\tilde{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{2}^{f}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}]\geq c\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{f}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{g}}_{2}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[\|\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g})\|_{2}^{2}]=c\tau_{g_{2}}^{2}(1-\rho_{g}^{2})\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}\|_{2}^{2}]\geq cp/n_{k}
, where we have used that
|\rho_{g}|\leq 1-c
.

For the
\alpha
-smoothed Lasso,
observe that for any
j\in[p]
,

\mathbb{P}\big([{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{f}]_{j}>2\lambda/(\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\zeta_{2})\;\text{and}\;[{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\tilde{\boldsymbol{g}}_{2}^{f}]_{j}<-2\lambda/(\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\zeta_{2})\big)>c.

(298)

Indeed, this event is implied by the simultaneous occurrence of
[{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{f}]_{j}\in[2\lambda/(\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\zeta_{2}),3\lambda/(\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\zeta_{2})]
and
\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}^{\perp}[{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}]_{j}<-5\lambda/(\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\zeta_{2})
.
Because
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
has bounded singular values and
\rho_{g}^{\perp}>c
,
the variance of both
[{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{f}]_{j}
and
\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}^{\perp}[{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}]_{j}
are bounded below and above by
c/n_{k}
and
C/n_{k}
.
Because further
\lambda
and
\zeta_{2}
are bounded above and below by a constant,
we conclude that each of these events has probability lower bounded by
c>0
.
Because
[{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{f}]_{j}
and
\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}^{\perp}[{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{g}]_{j}
are independent, we conclude the previous display.
Using Eq. (
296
) and the fact that
[\partial\Omega_{k}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}^{f})]_{j}\in[-\lambda/\sqrt{n_{k}},\lambda/\sqrt{n_{k}}]
for the
\alpha
-smoothed Lasso,
we conclude that on the event in the previous display,
\big|[{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2})]_{j}-[{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}(\tilde{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{2}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2})]_{j}\big|>2\lambda/(\sqrt{n_{2}}\zeta_{2})
.
Summing over
j
and using the the singular values of
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
are bounded below,
we conclude that

\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{f}-\tilde{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{2}^{f}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}]\geq c\mathbb{E}[\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}-\tilde{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{2}^{f})\|_{2}^{2}]\geq cp\frac{4\lambda^{2}}{n_{2}\zeta_{2}^{2}}\geq cp/n_{2}.

(299)

Thus, we have established Eq. (
297
) in all cases (i.e., least-squares, ridge regression, and the
\alpha
-smoothed Lasso).

Now we use the triangle inequality to get
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{f}-\tilde{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{2}^{f}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}\leq 2\|(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2})-(\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}/\tau_{h_{1}})(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1})\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}+2\|(\tilde{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{2}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2})-(\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}/\tau_{h_{1}})(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1})\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}
, and that
\mathbb{E}[\|(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2})-(\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}/\tau_{h_{1}})(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1})\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[\|(\tilde{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{2}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2})-(\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}/\tau_{h_{1}})(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1})\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}]
, to get

\displaystyle\frac{cp}{n_{2}}\leq\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{f}-\tilde{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{2}^{f}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq 4\mathbb{E}\big[\big\|(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2})-(\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}/\tau_{h_{1}})(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1})\big\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}\big]=\tau_{h_{2}}^{2}(1-\rho_{h}^{2})\leq\frac{Cp}{n_{2}}(1-\rho_{h}^{2}).

(300)

We conclude
|\rho_{h}|\leq 1-c
, as claimed.

Finally, by Eq. (
39
) and because proximal operators are 1-Lipschitz,

{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\eta_{k}({\boldsymbol{y}}_{k}^{f};\zeta_{k})
is
1
-Lipschitz in
{\boldsymbol{y}}_{k}^{f}
.
Thus,
\operatorname{div}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\eta_{k}({\boldsymbol{y}}_{k}^{f};\zeta_{k})\leq p
almost surely, so by Eq. (
41
),
\mathsf{df}_{k}\leq p
.
∎

Proof of Lemmas
E.7
and
F.5
.

We prove Lemmas
E.7
and
F.5
simultaneously, because both proofs involve coupling the fixed design models corresponding to two different regression estimators.

We compare two settings: one in which the second regression uses the Lasso with regularization parameter
\lambda
, and one in which the second regression uses the
\alpha
-smoothed Lasso with regularization parameter
\lambda
.
Across the the two settings, all other model parameters and the estimators in the first regression are the same.
Each setting gives rise to a set of solutions to the fixed-point equations (
42
) and corresponding fixed design model (
38
).
We will couple the fixed design models from the two settings so that the estimates from the second regression are close.

We introduce some notation.
The function
\mathsf{R}
appearing in the fixed point equations (
42
) will be denoted
\mathsf{R}^{(0)}
and
\mathsf{R}^{(\alpha)}
in the model in which the Lasso and
\alpha
-smoothed Lasso is used in the second regression, respectively.
Likewise we define
\mathsf{df}_{k}^{(0)}
and
\mathsf{df}_{k}^{(\alpha)}
.
The solutions to the fixed point equations, in the notation of Section
C.1
,
will be denoted
\tau_{g_{1},0},\tau_{g_{2},0},\rho_{g,0},\zeta_{1,0},\zeta_{2,0}
for the Lasso and
\tau_{g_{1},\alpha},\tau_{g_{2},\alpha},\rho_{g,\alpha},\zeta_{1,\alpha},\zeta_{2,\alpha}
for the
\alpha
-smoothed Lasso.
We will denote by
\tau_{g_{1}},\tau_{g_{2}},\rho_{g},\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}
(i.e., with the second subscript omitted) generic arguments to the functions
\mathsf{R}^{(0)},\mathsf{df}_{k}^{(0)},\mathsf{R}^{(\alpha)},\mathsf{df}_{k}^{(\alpha)}
which do not necessarily satisfy the fixed point equations (
42
) (or equivalently, (
57
) and (
58
)).
Note that because
\tau_{g_{1},0},\zeta_{1,0}
and
\tau_{g_{1},\alpha},\zeta_{1,\alpha}
are the solutions to the same equations (
57
) for
k=1
, we have
\tau_{g_{1},0}=\tau_{g_{1},\alpha}
and
\zeta_{1,0}=\zeta_{1,\alpha}
.
Denote the noise and estimates from the fixed design model in which the Lasso is used in the second regression by
\boldsymbol{g}_{1,0}^{f},\boldsymbol{g}_{2,0}^{f},\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1,0}^{f},\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,0}^{f}
,
and denote by
\boldsymbol{g}_{1,\alpha}^{f},\boldsymbol{g}_{2,\alpha}^{f},\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1,\alpha}^{f},\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,\alpha}^{f}
the corresponding objects for the fixed design model in which the
\alpha
-smoothed Lasso is used.

We now construct a coupling between the two fixed design models.
First, define
\boldsymbol{g}_{2,*}^{f}=(\tau_{g_{2},\alpha}/\tau_{g_{2},0})\boldsymbol{g}_{2,0}^{f}
and

\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,*}^{f}:=\argmin_{\boldsymbol{\pi}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}}\Big\{\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{g}_{2,*}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\boldsymbol{\pi}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2})\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{\zeta_{2,\alpha}}\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\mathsf{M}_{\alpha/\sqrt{n_{2}}}(\boldsymbol{\pi})\Big\}.

(301)

Note that
(\boldsymbol{g}_{2,*}^{f},\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,*}^{f})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{d}}}{{=}}(\boldsymbol{g}_{2,\alpha}^{f},\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,\alpha}^{f})
.
The quantity
\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,*}^{f}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,0}^{f}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}]
corresponds to the quantity
\|\boldsymbol{v}_{\alpha}^{*}-\boldsymbol{v}_{0}^{*}\|_{L_{2}}^{2}/n
in the proof Lemma A.5 of
[
CMW20
]
.
There it is shown that this quantity is bounded by
C\alpha
for
\alpha<c^{\prime}
.
Moreover, Lemma A.5 of
[
CMW20
]
states that
|\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\tau_{g_{2},\alpha}-\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\tau_{g_{2},0}|<C\sqrt{\alpha}
for
\alpha<c^{\prime}
,
whence, using Lemma
C.1
, we conclude that
|\tau_{g_{2},\alpha}/\tau_{g_{2},0}-1|<C\sqrt{\alpha}
.
We conclude that
\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{g}_{2,*}^{f}-\boldsymbol{g}_{2,0}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]<C\alpha p/n_{2}<C\alpha
.
In particular,
for any
1
-Lipschitz function
\phi_{\theta}:({\mathbb{R}}^{p})^{2}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
,
we have

\big|\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\theta}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,\alpha}^{f},\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,\alpha}^{f,\mathrm{d}})]-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\theta}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,0}^{f},\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,0}^{f,\mathrm{d}})]\big|<C\sqrt{\alpha}.

(302)

Lemma
E.7
follows by applying this bound to
\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\theta}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}^{f},\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}^{f,\mathrm{d}})]
for
\alpha^{\prime}\leq\alpha
and using the triangle inequality and
C\sqrt{\alpha^{\prime}}+C\sqrt{\alpha}\leq C\sqrt{\alpha}
.

Next observe that

\displaystyle\big|\mathsf{R}_{12}^{(\alpha)}(\tau_{g_{1},0},\tau_{g_{2},\alpha},\rho_{g,0},\zeta_{1,0},\zeta_{2,\alpha})-\mathsf{R}_{12}^{(0)}(\tau_{g_{1},0},\tau_{g_{2},0},\rho_{g,0},\zeta_{1,0},\zeta_{2,0})\big|

(303)

\displaystyle=\Big|\mathbb{E}[\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1,0}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1},\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,*}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}\rangle_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1,0}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1},\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,0}^{f}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}\rangle_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}]\Big|\leq C\tau_{h_{1}}\sqrt{\alpha}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\alpha p}{n_{1}}}.

Recall that
\tau_{g_{1},0}=\tau_{g_{1},\alpha}
and
\zeta_{2,0}=\zeta_{2,\alpha}
.
Using the fixed point equation (
58
),
we conclude that

\Big|\tau_{g_{1},\alpha}\tau_{g_{2},0}\rho_{g,0}-\frac{n_{12}}{n_{1}n_{2}}\big(\tau_{e_{1}}\tau_{e_{2}}\rho_{e}+\mathsf{R}_{12}^{(\alpha)}(\tau_{g_{1},\alpha},\tau_{g_{2},\alpha},\rho_{g,0},\zeta_{1,\alpha},\zeta_{2,\alpha})\big)\Big|\leq C\frac{n_{12}}{n_{1}n_{2}}\sqrt{\frac{p}{n_{1}}}\,\sqrt{\alpha}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{n_{1}n_{2}}},

(304)

where in the last inequality we use that
p/n_{1}\leq C
and
n_{12}\leq\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}
.
Because
\big|\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\tau_{g_{2},\alpha}-\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\tau_{g_{2},0}\big|<C\sqrt{\alpha}
and
\tau_{g_{1},\alpha}\leq C/\sqrt{n_{1}}
,
we conclude that

\Big|\tau_{g_{1},\alpha}\tau_{g_{2},\alpha}\rho_{g,0}-\frac{n_{12}}{n_{1}n_{2}}\big(\tau_{e_{1}}\tau_{e_{2}}\rho_{e}+\mathsf{R}_{12}^{(\alpha)}(\tau_{g_{1},\alpha},\tau_{g_{2},\alpha},\rho_{g,0},\zeta_{1,\alpha},\zeta_{2,\alpha})\big)\Big|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{n_{1}n_{2}}}.

(305)

Finally, as argued in the proof of Lemma
6.1
(see Eq. (
292
)),
we have

\displaystyle\frac{n_{12}}{n_{1}n_{2}}\big|\partial_{\rho_{g}}\mathsf{R}_{12}^{(\alpha)}(\tau_{g_{1},\alpha},\tau_{g_{2},\alpha},\rho_{g},\zeta_{1,\alpha},\zeta_{2,\alpha})\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\tau_{g_{1},\alpha}\tau_{g_{2},\alpha}\frac{n_{12}}{n_{1}n_{2}}\sqrt{\mathsf{df}_{1}^{(\alpha)}(\tau_{g_{1},\alpha}^{2},\zeta_{1,\alpha})\mathsf{df}_{2}^{(\alpha)}(\tau_{g_{2},\alpha}^{2},\zeta_{2,\alpha})}

(306)

\displaystyle\leq\tau_{g_{1},\alpha}\tau_{g_{2},\alpha}(1-c),

where we use that

\mathsf{df}_{k}^{(\alpha)}(\tau_{g_{k},\alpha}^{2},\zeta_{k,\alpha})/n_{k}\leq 1-c
by Lemma
C.1
.
We see that the derivative of the term in the absolute value in Eq. (
305
) has partial derivative with respect to
\rho_{g,0}
no smaller than
c\tau_{g_{1},\alpha}\tau_{g_{2},\alpha}
.
Because
\tau_{g_{1},\alpha}\geq c/\sqrt{n_{1}}
and
\tau_{g_{2},\alpha}\geq c/\sqrt{n_{2}}
by Lemma
C.1
,
Eq. (
305
) and the derivative bound imply
\big|\rho_{g,\alpha}-\rho_{g,0}\big|<C\sqrt{\alpha}
.
Because
\rho_{g,\alpha}^{\perp}=\sqrt{1-\rho_{g,\alpha}^{2}}
and
\rho_{g,0}^{\perp}=\sqrt{1-\rho_{g,0}^{2}}
,
and
|\rho_{g,\alpha}|,|\rho_{g,0}|<1-c
by Lemma
C.1
,
we conclude also that
|\rho_{g,\alpha}^{\perp}-\rho_{g,0}^{\perp}|<C\sqrt{\alpha}
.

We now construct the coupling between
(\boldsymbol{g}_{1,0}^{f},\boldsymbol{g}_{2,0}^{f})
and
(\boldsymbol{g}_{1,\alpha}^{f},\boldsymbol{g}_{2,\alpha}^{f})
.
Note that we may represent
\boldsymbol{g}_{2,0}^{f}=(\tau_{g_{2},0}\rho_{g,0}/\tau_{g_{1},0})\boldsymbol{g}_{1,0}^{f}+\tau_{g_{2},0}\rho_{g,0}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}
, where
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{p})
independent of everything else.
Using this representation, we construct the coupling
\boldsymbol{g}_{1,\alpha}^{f}=\boldsymbol{g}_{1,0}^{f}
and
\boldsymbol{g}_{2,\alpha}^{f}=(\tau_{g_{2},\alpha}\rho_{g,\alpha}/\tau_{g_{1},\alpha})\boldsymbol{g}_{1,0}^{f}+\tau_{g_{2},\alpha}\rho_{g,\alpha}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}
.
Note that

\|\boldsymbol{g}_{2,\alpha}^{f}-\boldsymbol{g}_{2,*}^{f}\|_{2}\leq\|(\tau_{g_{2},\alpha}/\tau_{g_{1},\alpha})\boldsymbol{g}_{1,0}^{f}\|_{2}|\rho_{g,\alpha}-\rho_{g,0}|+\|\tau_{g_{2},\alpha}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}\|_{2}|\rho_{g,\alpha}^{\perp}-\rho_{g,0}^{\perp}|.

(307)

Using the bounds on
\big|\rho_{g,\alpha}-\rho_{g,0}\big|
and
|\rho_{g,\alpha}^{\perp}-\rho_{g,0}^{\perp}|
above and the bounds on
\tau_{g_{2},\alpha}
and
\tau_{g_{2},0}
from Lemma
C.1
,
we conclude that
\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{g}_{2,\alpha}^{f}-\boldsymbol{g}_{2,*}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq C\alpha
for
\alpha<c^{\prime}
.
Because proximal operators are
1
-Lipschitz,
we then also conclude that
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,\alpha}^{f}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,*}^{f}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}]\leq C\alpha
.
We have already shown that
\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,*}^{f}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,0}^{f}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}]<C\alpha
and
\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{g}_{2,*}^{f}-\boldsymbol{g}_{2,0}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]<C\alpha
.
Thus,
we have that
\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,\alpha}^{f}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2,0}^{f}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}]<C\alpha
and
\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{g}_{2,\alpha}^{f}-\boldsymbol{g}_{2,0}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]<C\alpha
under this coupling.

Using this coupling, we can derive the conclusion of the lemma.
As argued in the proof of Lemma
D.2
(see Section
J.2
),

\phi_{\theta|1}^{(\alpha)}
and
\phi_{\theta|1}^{(0)}
can be viewed as a function of
\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1}
.
Writing it as a function of this argument and computing expectations under this coupling, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\phi_{\theta|1}^{(\alpha)}(\boldsymbol{g}_{1,0}^{f})-\phi_{\theta|1}^{(0)}(\boldsymbol{g}_{1,0}^{f})\big|\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\mathbb{E}\big[\phi_{\theta}(\{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k,\alpha}^{f}\},\{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k,\alpha}^{f,\mathrm{d}}\})\bigm|\boldsymbol{g}_{1,0}^{f}\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\phi_{\theta}(\{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k,0}^{f}\},\{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k,0}^{f,\mathrm{d}}\})\bigm|\boldsymbol{g}_{1,0}^{f}\big]\big|\big]

(308)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\phi_{\theta}(\{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k,\alpha}^{f}\},\{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k,\alpha}^{f,\mathrm{d}}\})-\phi_{\theta}(\{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k,0}^{f}\},\{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k,0}^{f,\mathrm{d}}\})\big|\big]\leq CM_{2}\sqrt{\alpha}.

As argued in the proof of Lemma
D.2
(see Section
J.2
),

\phi_{\theta|1}^{(\alpha)}
and
\phi_{\theta|1}^{(0)}
are
C(M_{1}+M_{2}\sqrt{n_{1}/n_{2}})
-Lipschitz in
\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1}
,
whence
\big|\phi_{\theta|1}^{(\alpha)}(\cdot)-\phi_{\theta|1}^{(0)}(\cdot)\big|
is
C(M_{1}+M_{2}\sqrt{n_{1}/n_{2}})
-Lipschitz.
By Corollary
J.2
,
we have for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
with probability at least
1-\mathsf{C}(\epsilon)e^{-\mathsf{c}(\epsilon)p}

that

\Big|\big|\phi_{\theta|1}^{(\alpha)}(\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1})-\phi_{\theta|1}^{(0)}(\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1})\big|-\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\phi_{\theta|1}^{(\alpha)}(\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1})-\phi_{\theta|1}^{(0)}(\hat{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1})\big|\big]\Big|<C(M_{1}\sqrt{p/n_{1}}+M_{2}\sqrt{p/n_{2}})\,\epsilon\leq C(M_{1}\sqrt{p/n_{1}}+M_{2})\epsilon.

(309)

Taking
\epsilon=\sqrt{\alpha}
gives Lemma
F.5
for
\alpha^{\prime}=0
.
Lemma
F.5
for
\alpha^{\prime}\leq\alpha
follows by applying the previous display with
\alpha^{\prime}
in place of
\alpha
and using the triangle inequality and
\sqrt{\alpha^{\prime}}+\sqrt{\alpha}\leq 2\sqrt{\alpha}
.
∎

M.2
Gram-Schmidt approximation bound

Lemma M.2
.

Consider
\boldsymbol{A}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times k}
such that
\big\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}-\mathsf{T}\big\|_{\mathsf{F}}<\epsilon
.
Denoting the columns of
\boldsymbol{A}
by
\boldsymbol{a}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{a}_{k}
, assume also that
\mathsf{T}
has singular values bounded below and above by
c
and
C
respectively.
Consider applying the Gram-Schmidt procedure the
\boldsymbol{a}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{a}_{k}
in that order, to produce
\boldsymbol{a}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}},\ldots,\boldsymbol{a}_{k}^{\mathrm{gs}}
which satisfy
\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}\top}\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}=\mathsf{T}
.
Then there exists constants
C^{\prime},c^{\prime}>0
depending only on
c,C,k
such that for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}

\max_{j\in[k]}\Big\{\|\boldsymbol{a}_{j}^{\mathrm{gs}}-\boldsymbol{a}_{j}\|_{2}\Big\}<C^{\prime}\epsilon.

(310)

Proof of Lemma
M.2
.

We denote the matrix in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times j}
formed by the first
j
columns of
\boldsymbol{A}
by
\boldsymbol{A}(j)
, and the submatrix of
\mathsf{T}
formed by the first
j
rows and columns by
\mathsf{T}(j)
.
We prove the result inductively.
Throughout the proof,
C^{\prime},c^{\prime}
may change at each appearance, but only depend on
c,C,k
.

For the base case, observe
\boldsymbol{a}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}=\sqrt{\mathsf{T}_{11}}\boldsymbol{a}_{1}/\|\boldsymbol{a}_{1}\|_{2}
. Because
\mathsf{T}_{11}>c
, for
\epsilon<c^{\prime}
we have
\big|\sqrt{\mathsf{T}_{11}}/\|\boldsymbol{a}_{1}\|_{2}-1\big|<C^{\prime}\epsilon
and
\|\boldsymbol{a}_{1}\|_{2}\leq C^{\prime}
.
Thus,
\|\boldsymbol{a}_{1}^{\mathrm{gs}}-\boldsymbol{a}_{1}\|_{2}<C^{\prime}\epsilon
.

Now assume the inductive hypothesis, that
\max_{j\leq i}\|\boldsymbol{a}_{j}^{\mathrm{gs}}-\boldsymbol{a}_{j}\|_{2}<C^{\prime}\epsilon
.
We have

\boldsymbol{a}^{\mathrm{gs}}_{i+1}=\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)\mathsf{T}(i)^{-1}\mathsf{T}_{1:i,i+1}+\sqrt{T_{i+1,i+1}}\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}\|_{2}},

(311)

and

\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}=\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)}\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}+\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}=\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)\mathsf{T}(i)^{-1}\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}+\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}.

(312)

Note

\big\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}-\mathsf{T}_{1:i,i+1}\big\|_{2}\leq\big\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}-\boldsymbol{A}(i)^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}\big\|_{2}+\big\|\boldsymbol{A}(i)^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}-\mathsf{T}_{1:i,i+1}\big\|_{2}<C^{\prime}\epsilon,

(313)

whence because
\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)\mathsf{T}(i)^{-1}\|_{\mathsf{op}}<C^{\prime}
,
we conclude

\big\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)\mathsf{T}(i)^{-1}\mathsf{T}_{1:i,i+1}-\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)}\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}\big\|_{2}<C^{\prime}\epsilon.

(314)

This implies that

\Big|\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)}\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{gs}}\big\|_{2}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)}\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}\big\|_{2}\Big|=\Big|\mathsf{T}_{i+1,1:i}\mathsf{T}(i)^{-1}\mathsf{T}_{1:i,i+1}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)}\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}\big\|_{2}\Big|<C^{\prime}\epsilon.

(315)

Using the previous display and that
\big|\|\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}\|_{2}^{2}-T_{i+1,i+1}\big|<\epsilon
,
we have

\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}\|_{2}=\sqrt{\|\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)}\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}^{2}\|}

differs from
\sqrt{\mathsf{T}_{i+1,i+1}-\mathsf{T}_{i+1,1:i}\mathsf{T}(i)^{-1}\mathsf{T}_{1:i,i+1}}
by at most
C^{\prime}\epsilon
,
where we use that
\mathsf{T}_{i+1,i+1}-\mathsf{T}_{i+1,1:i}\mathsf{T}(i)^{-1}\mathsf{T}_{1:i,i+1}>c
because
\mathsf{T}
has singular values bounded below by
c
.
Thus,

\Big|\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{gs}}\|_{2}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}\|_{2}\Big|=\Big|\sqrt{\mathsf{T}_{i+1,i+1}-\mathsf{T}_{i+1,1:i}\mathsf{T}(i)^{-1}\mathsf{T}_{1:i,i+1}}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{A}^{\mathrm{gs}}(i)}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}\|_{2}\Big|<C^{\prime}\epsilon.

(316)

Combined with Eq. (
315
), we conclude
\|\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}^{\mathrm{gs}}-\boldsymbol{a}_{i+1}\|_{2}<C^{\prime}\epsilon
, and the inductive step is complete.
∎

M.3
Comparison of cosines

We prove the following lemma.

Lemma M.3
.

For a positive definite matrix
\boldsymbol{K}
, define
\cos_{\boldsymbol{K}}(\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b})=\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}\boldsymbol{b}/(\|\boldsymbol{a}\|_{\boldsymbol{K}}\|\boldsymbol{b}\|_{\boldsymbol{K}})
,
and define
\sin_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{2}(\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b})=1-\cos_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{2}(\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b})
.
Let
\sigma_{\max}(\boldsymbol{K}),\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{K})
, and
\kappa_{\mathrm{cond}}(\boldsymbol{K})
be the maximal and minimal singular values and the condition number of
\boldsymbol{K}
respectively.
Then for any
\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}

1-\cos_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{2}(\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b})\geq\frac{1-\cos_{\mathbf{I}}^{2}(\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b})}{4\kappa_{\mathrm{cond}}(\boldsymbol{K})}.

(317)

Proof of Lemma
M.3
.

Because the cosine is invariant to scaling
\boldsymbol{a}
and
\boldsymbol{b}
, we may without loss of generality assume
\|\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}=\|\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}=1
.
Then

\displaystyle\cos_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{2}(\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b})

\displaystyle=\cos_{\mathbf{I}}^{2}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{K}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{a}}{\|\boldsymbol{K}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}},\frac{\boldsymbol{K}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{b}}{\|\boldsymbol{K}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}}\Big)=1-\frac{1}{4}\Big\|\frac{\boldsymbol{K}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{a}}{\|\boldsymbol{K}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}}-\frac{\boldsymbol{K}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{b}}{\|\boldsymbol{K}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}}\Big\|_{2}^{2}

(318)

\displaystyle\leq 1-\frac{\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{K})}{4}\Big\|\frac{\boldsymbol{a}}{\|\boldsymbol{K}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}}-\frac{\boldsymbol{b}}{\|\boldsymbol{K}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}}\Big\|_{2}^{2}\leq 1-\frac{\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{K})}{4}\Big\|\frac{\boldsymbol{a}}{\|\boldsymbol{K}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}}-\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\boldsymbol{b}}\boldsymbol{a}}{\|\boldsymbol{K}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}}\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{K})\sin_{\mathbf{I}}(\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b})^{2}}{4\|\boldsymbol{K}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}^{2}}\leq 1-\frac{\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{K})\sin_{\mathbf{I}}(\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b})^{2}}{4\sigma_{\max}(\boldsymbol{K})}=1-\frac{1-\cos_{\mathbf{I}}^{2}(\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b})}{4\kappa_{\mathrm{cond}}(\boldsymbol{K})}.

The proof is complete.
∎

M.4
Change of normalization from
[
CMW20
]

Our results rely on the lasso characterization provided by
[
CMW20
]
, which uses a different normalization: in our case the covariates are
{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{iid}}}{{\sim}}\mathsf{N}(0,{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})
, whereas in
[
CMW20
]
the covariates are
{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{iid}}}{{\sim}}\mathsf{N}(0,{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}/n_{\cdot})
.
We here provide the change of variables to tranfer results between the two papers.

We will denote quantities which occur in their paper with a prime.
They consider a design matrix
{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\prime}
, observation
{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\prime}
, true parameter
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\prime}
, noise standard deviation
\sigma^{\prime}
, Lasso regularization
\lambda^{\prime}
, and fixed-point parameter
\tau^{\prime},\zeta^{\prime}
.
These are related to the quantites in the present paper by

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{\mathsf{x}}}}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}},\qquad

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle={\boldsymbol{y}}_{k},\qquad

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n_{k}}\,{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k},\qquad

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle={\boldsymbol{\Sigma}},\qquad

\displaystyle\alpha^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{k},

(319)

\displaystyle\sigma^{\prime 2}

\displaystyle=\tau_{e_{k}}^{2},\qquad

\displaystyle\lambda^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{k},\qquad

\displaystyle\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n_{k}}\,\tau_{g_{k}},\qquad

\displaystyle\zeta^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\zeta_{k}.

With some algebra, one can check that the fixed point system equations (8) from
[
CMW20
]
is equivalent to the outcome regression equations and precision regression equations in Eq. (
42
) in the present paper.

M.5
Reference on second moment identities

We write explicitly the expectations appearing in Eq. (
162
). They are

\begin{gathered}\mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{N},k}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\boldsymbol{T}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}\Big)\Bigm|\boldsymbol{e}_{k}\Big]=\begin{pmatrix}\tau_{e_{k}}^{*2}&0\\[2.0pt]
0&1\end{pmatrix},\\
\mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{P},k}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\boldsymbol{T}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{n_{k}}{p}}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f},\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}\Big)\Big]=\begin{pmatrix}(n_{k}/p)\tau_{h_{k}}^{2}&\sqrt{n_{k}}\,\tau_{g_{k}}\mathsf{df}_{k}/p\\[2.0pt]
\sqrt{n_{k}}\,\tau_{g_{k}}\mathsf{df}_{k}/p&1\end{pmatrix},\\
\text{and $\mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{P},k}$ has entries bounded above by $C$,}\end{gathered}

(320)

which we now check.
Because
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{n_{k}})
is independent of
\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}
and
\tau_{e_{k}}^{*}=\|\boldsymbol{e}_{k,\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n_{k}}
by definition,
the expression of
\mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{N},k}
is easily checked.
By the definition of
\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f}
(see Eq. (
81
)) and the identity Eq. (
61
), we have
\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]=\tau_{h_{k}}^{2}
. Because
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{p})
, we have
\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{g}\|_{2}^{2}]/p=1
.
Thus, the diagonal entries of
\mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{P},k}
are as stated.
By the definition of
\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f}
(see Eqs. (
39
) and (
81
)) and
because proximal operators are 1-Lipschitz,
we see that
\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f}
is
1
-Lipschitz in
\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}
.
Thus, we may apply Gaussian integration by parts,
which gives
\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f\top}\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f}]=\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}\mathsf{df}_{k}
by the definition of
\mathsf{df}_{k}
(see Eq. (
41
)) and because
\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\tau_{g_{k}}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{p})
.
Thus, the off-diagonal entries of
\mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{P},k}
are as stated.
By Lemma
C.1
,
(n_{k}/p)\tau_{h_{k}}^{2}\leq C
, so Eq. (
320
) follows.

We also rewrite explicitly the expectations appearing in Eq. (
162
).
They are

\begin{gathered}\begin{aligned} \mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{N},2|1}&=\mathbb{E}\Big[\boldsymbol{T}\Big(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}}{\sqrt{n_{1}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\tau_{h_{1}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{h}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}-(\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}/\tau_{h_{1}})\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}}{\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}^{\perp}}\Big)\Big]\\
&=\begin{pmatrix}\tau_{e_{2}}^{2}&\frac{n_{12}}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}\tau_{e_{1}}\tau_{e_{2}}\rho_{e}\zeta_{1}&0&0\\[4.0pt]
\frac{n_{12}}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}\tau_{e_{1}}\tau_{e_{2}}\rho_{e}\zeta_{1}&n_{12}\tau_{g_{1}}^{2}\zeta_{1}^{2}&\frac{n_{12}}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}\,\tau_{h_{1}}\zeta_{1}&0\\[4.0pt]
0&\frac{n_{12}}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}\,\tau_{h_{1}}\zeta_{1}&1&0\\[4.0pt]
0&0&0&1\end{pmatrix},\\[4.0pt]
\mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{P},2|1}&=\mathbb{E}\Big[\boldsymbol{T}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{n_{1}}{p}}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{f},\sqrt{\frac{n_{2}}{p}}\,\boldsymbol{v}_{2}^{f},\frac{\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f}}{\sqrt{p}\,\tau_{g_{1}}},\frac{\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{f}-(\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}/\tau_{g_{1}})\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f}}{\sqrt{p}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}^{\perp}}\Big)\Big]\\
&=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{n_{1}}{p}\tau_{h_{1}}^{2}&\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}{p}\tau_{h_{1}}\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}&\sqrt{n_{1}}\,\tau_{g_{1}}\frac{\mathsf{df}_{1}}{p}&0\\[4.0pt]
\frac{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}{p}\tau_{h_{1}}\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}&\frac{n_{2}}{p}\tau_{h_{2}}^{2}&\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}\frac{\mathsf{df}_{2}}{p}&\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}^{\perp}\frac{\mathsf{df}_{2}}{p}\\[4.0pt]
\sqrt{n_{1}}\,\tau_{g_{1}}\frac{\mathsf{df}_{1}}{p}&\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}\frac{\mathsf{df}_{2}}{p}&1&0\\[4.0pt]
0&\sqrt{n_{2}}\,\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}^{\perp}\frac{\mathsf{df}_{2}}{p}&0&1\end{pmatrix},\end{aligned}\\[4.0pt]
\text{and $\mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{N},2|1}$ and $\mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{P},2|1}$ have entries bounded above by $C$,}\end{gathered}

(321)

which we now check.
By Eqs. (
81
) and (
40
),
we have

\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}=\begin{pmatrix}\zeta_{1}(\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f})\\[4.0pt]
\boldsymbol{0}_{n_{2}-n_{12}}\end{pmatrix},

(322)

where with some abuse of notation, we have written those indices in
\mathcal{I}_{1}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}
first, and those in
\mathcal{I}_{2}\setminus\mathcal{I}_{1}
second.
This gives us that
\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\|_{2}^{2}]/n_{2}=\tau_{e_{2}}^{2}
,
\mathbb{E}[\langle\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}},\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}\rangle]/\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}=\zeta_{1}\mathbb{E}[\langle\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{1}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}},\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}\rangle]/\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}=(n_{12}/\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}})\tau_{e_{1}}\tau_{e_{2}}\rho_{e}\zeta_{1}
, and
\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]/n_{1}=\zeta_{1}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}]/n_{1}=\zeta_{1}^{2}n_{12}(\tau_{e_{1}}^{2}+\tau_{h_{1}}^{2})/n_{1}=n_{12}\tau_{g_{1}}^{2}\zeta_{1}^{2}
, where we have applied the independence of
\boldsymbol{e}_{2}
and
\boldsymbol{h}_{1}
and Eq. (
64
).
Note that
\tau_{e_{2}}^{2}\leq C
by
A1
4 and
n_{12}\tau_{g_{1}}^{2}\zeta_{1}^{2}\leq n_{1}\tau_{g_{1}}^{2}\leq C
by Lemma
C.1
.
Thus, we have computed the top left
2\times 2
block of
\mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{N},2|1}
.

By the definition of
\tau_{h_{1}},\tau_{h_{2}},\rho_{h}
(see Section
C.1
),
we have
\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}/\tau_{h_{1}}
and
(\boldsymbol{h}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}-(\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}/\tau_{h_{1}})\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f})/(\tau_{h_{2}}\rho_{h}^{\perp}
are standard Gaussian and independent, both are independent of
\boldsymbol{e}_{2,\mathcal{I}_{2}}
, and the latter is independent of
\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}
.
This gives the computation for the bottom right
2\times 2
block of
\mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{N},2|1}
.
For the top right
2\times 2
block of
\mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{N},2|1}
, the only non-zero entry comes from
\mathbb{E}[\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f},\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}\rangle]/(\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}\,\tau_{h_{1}})
, because the remaining entries are 0 by independence.
We compute
\mathbb{E}[\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f},\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}\rangle]/(\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}\,\tau_{h_{1}})=\zeta_{1}\mathbb{E}[\langle\boldsymbol{e}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}},\boldsymbol{h}_{1,\mathcal{I}_{1}\cap\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{f}\rangle]/(\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}\,\tau_{h_{1}})=(n_{12}/\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}})\tau_{h_{1}}\zeta_{1}
as claimed.

The top left
2\times 2
block of
\mathcal{T}_{\mathsf{P},2|1}
is computed using Eq. (
61
) and recalling that
\boldsymbol{v}_{k}={\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})
(see Eq. (
81
)).
By Lemma
C.1
,
(n_{k}/p)\tau_{h_{k}}^{2}\leq C
.
By the definition of
\tau_{g_{1}},\tau_{g_{2}},\rho_{g}
(see Section
C.1
),
we have
\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f}/\tau_{g_{1}}
and
(\boldsymbol{g}_{2}^{f}-(\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}/\tau_{g_{1}})\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{f})/(\tau_{g_{2}}\rho_{g}^{\perp})
are standard Gaussian and independent, which gives the bottom right block.
The top right block is computed using Gaussian integration by parts, recalling that because
\boldsymbol{v}_{k}={\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}(\eta_{k}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}+\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f};\zeta_{k})-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k})
(see Eqs. (
39
) and (
81
)), the definition of
\mathsf{df}_{k}
in Eq. (
41
) gives
\mathbb{E}[\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{l}^{f},\boldsymbol{v}_{k}^{f}\rangle]=\operatorname{Cov}(\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{f},\boldsymbol{g}_{l}^{f})\mathsf{df}_{k}
.
